> - <sup>g</sup> 23. Or, _established_
> - <sup>h</sup> 26. Heb. _all the day_
> - <sup>i</sup> 31. Or, _goings_
> - <sup>j</sup> 35. Or, _a green tree, that groweth in his own soil_

## Chapter 38

> _A Psalm of David, to bring to remembrance._

<sup>1</sup> O LORD, rebuke me not in thy wrath:<br>Neither chasten me in thy hot displeasure.<br>
<sup>2</sup> For thine arrows stick fast in me,<br>And thy hand presseth me sore.<br>
<sup>3</sup> _There is_ no soundness in my flesh because of thine anger;<br>Neither _is there any_ rest<sup>a</sup> in my bones because of my sin.<br>
<sup>4</sup> For mine iniquities are gone over mine head:<br>As an heavy burden they are too heavy for me.<br>
<sup>5</sup> My wounds stink _and_ are corrupt<br>Because of my foolishness.<br>
<sup>6</sup> I am troubled;<sup>b</sup> I am bowed down greatly;<br>I go mourning all the day long.<br>
<sup>7</sup> For my loins are filled with a loathsome _disease:_<br>And _there is_ no soundness in my flesh.<br>
<sup>8</sup> I am feeble and sore broken:<br>I have roared by reason of the disquietness of my heart.<br>
<sup>9</sup> Lord, all my desire _is_ before thee;<br>And my groaning is not hid from thee.<br>
<sup>10</sup> My heart panteth, my strength faileth me:<br>As for the light of mine eyes, it also is gone from me.<sup>c</sup><br>
<sup>11</sup> My lovers and my friends stand aloof from my sore;<sup>d</sup><br>And my kinsmen<sup>e</sup> stand afar off.<br>
<sup>12</sup> They also that seek after my life lay snares _for me:_<br>And they that seek my hurt speak mischievous things, and imagine deceits all the day long.<br>
<sup>13</sup> But I, as a deaf _man_, heard not;<br>And _I was_ as a dumb man _that_ openeth not his mouth.<br>
<sup>14</sup> Thus I was as a man that heareth not,<br>And in whose mouth _are_ no reproofs.<br>
<sup>15</sup> For in thee,<sup>f</sup> O LORD, do I hope:<br>Thou wilt hear,<sup>g</sup> O Lord my God.<br>
<sup>16</sup> For I said, _Hear me_, lest _otherwise_ they should rejoice over me:<br>When my foot slippeth, they magnify _themselves_ against me.<br>
<sup>17</sup> For I _am_ ready to halt,<sup>h</sup><br>And my sorrow _is_ continually before me.<br>
<sup>18</sup> For I will declare mine iniquity;<br>I will be sorry for my sin.<br>
<sup>19</sup> But mine enemies _are_ lively,<sup>i</sup> _and_ they are strong:<br>And they that hate me wrongfully are multiplied.<br>
<sup>20</sup> They also that render evil for good<br>Are mine adversaries; because I follow _the thing that_ good _is_.<br>
<sup>21</sup> Forsake me not, O LORD:<br>O my God, be not far from me.<br>
<sup>22</sup> Make haste to help me,<br>O Lord my salvation.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 3. Heb. _peace_, or _health_
> - <sup>b</sup> 6. Heb. _worried_
> - <sup>c</sup> 10. Heb. _is not with me_
> - <sup>d</sup> 11. Heb. _stroke_
> - <sup>e</sup> 11. Or, _my neighbors_
> - <sup>f</sup> 15. Or, _thee do I wait for_
> - <sup>g</sup> 15. Or, _answer_
> - <sup>h</sup> 17. Heb. _for halting_
> - <sup>i</sup> 19. Heb. _being living, are strong_

## Chapter 39

> _To the chief Musician, _even_ to Jeduthun, A Psalm of David._

<sup>1</sup> I said, I will take heed to my ways,<br>That I sin not with my tongue:<br>I will keep my mouth with a bridle,<sup>a</sup><br>While the wicked is before me.<br>
<sup>2</sup> I was dumb with silence, I held my peace, _even_ from good;<br>And my sorrow was stirred.<sup>b</sup><br>
<sup>3</sup> My heart was hot within me, while I was musing the fire burned:<br>_Then_ spake I with my tongue.<br>
<sup>4</sup> LORD, make me to know mine end, and the measure of my days, what it _is_;<br>_That_ I may know how frail<sup>c</sup> I _am_.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Behold, thou hast made my days _as_ an handbreadth;<br>And mine age _is_ as nothing before thee: Verily every man at his best state<sup>d</sup> _is_ altogether vanity. _Selah._<br>
<sup>6</sup> Surely every man walketh in a vain shew: Surely they are disquieted in vain:<br>He heapeth up _riches_, and knoweth not who shall gather them.<br>
<sup>7</sup> And now, Lord, what wait I for?<br>My hope _is_ in thee.<br>
<sup>8</sup> Deliver me from all my transgressions:<br>Make me not the reproach of the foolish.<br>
<sup>9</sup> I was dumb, I opened not my mouth;<br>Because thou didst _it_.
<sup>10</sup> Remove thy stroke away from me:<br>I am consumed by the blow<sup>e</sup> of thine hand.<br>
<sup>11</sup> When thou with rebukes dost correct man for iniquity,<br>Thou makest his beauty to consume away<sup>f</sup> like a moth:<br>Surely every man _is_ vanity. _Selah._<br>
<sup>12</sup> Hear my prayer, O LORD, and give ear unto my cry;<br>Hold not thy peace at my tears:<br>For I _am_ a stranger with thee,<br>_And_ a sojourner, as all my fathers _were_.<br>
<sup>13</sup> O spare me, that I may recover strength,<br>Before I go hence, and be no more.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Heb. _a bridle_, or _muzzle for my mouth_
> - <sup>b</sup> 2. Heb. _troubled_
> - <sup>c</sup> 4. Or, _what time I have here_
> - <sup>d</sup> 5. Heb. _settled_
> - <sup>e</sup> 10. Heb. _conflict_
> - <sup>f</sup> 11. Heb. _that which is to be desired in him, to melt away_

## Chapter 40

> _To the chief Musician, A Psalm of David._

<sup>1</sup> I waited patiently<sup>a</sup> for the LORD;<br>And he inclined unto me, and heard my cry.<br>
<sup>2</sup> He brought me up also out of an horrible pit,<sup>b</sup> out of the miry clay,<br>And set my feet upon a rock, _and_ established my goings.<br>
<sup>3</sup> And he hath put a new song in my mouth, _even_<br>praise unto our God:<br>Many shall see _it_, and fear,<br>And shall trust in the LORD.<br>
<sup>4</sup> Blessed _is_ that man that maketh the LORD his trust,<br>And respecteth not the proud, nor such as turn aside to lies.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Many, O LORD my God,<br>_Are_ thy wonderful works _which_ thou hast done,<br>And thy thoughts _which are_ to us-ward:<br>They cannot be reckoned up in order unto thee:<sup>c</sup><br>_If_ I would declare and speak _of them_,<br>They are more than can be numbered.<br>
<sup>6</sup> Sacrifice and offering thou didst not desire;<br>Mine ears hast thou opened:<sup>d</sup><br>Burnt offering and sin offering hast thou not required.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Then said I, Lo, I come:<br>In the volume of the book _it is_ written of me,
<sup>8</sup> I delight to do thy will, O my God:<br>Yea, thy law _is_ within my heart.<sup>e</sup><br>
<sup>9</sup> I have preached righteousness in the great congregation:<br>Lo, I have not refrained my lips, O LORD, thou knowest.<br>
<sup>10</sup> I have not hid thy righteousness within my heart;<br>I have declared thy faithfulness and thy salvation:<br>I have not concealed thy lovingkindness,<br>And thy truth from the great congregation.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Withhold not thou thy tender mercies from me, O LORD:<br>Let thy lovingkindness and thy truth continually preserve me.<br>
<sup>12</sup> For innumerable evils have compassed me about:<br>Mine iniquities have taken hold upon me, so that I am not able to look up;<br>They are more than the hairs of mine head:<br>Therefore my heart faileth<sup>f</sup> me.<br>
<sup>13</sup> Be pleased, O LORD, to deliver me:<br>O LORD, make haste to help me.<br>
<sup>14</sup> Let them be ashamed and confounded together<br>That seek after my soul to destroy it;<br>Let them be driven backward<br>and put to shame that wish me evil.<br>
<sup>15</sup> Let them be desolate for a reward of their shame<br>That say unto me, Aha, aha.<br>
<sup>16</sup> Let all those that seek thee rejoice and be glad in thee:<br>Let such as love thy salvation say continually, the LORD be magnified.<br>
<sup>17</sup> But I _am_ poor and needy; _Yet_ the Lord thinketh upon me:<br>Thou _art_ my help and my deliverer; Make no tarrying, O my God.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Heb. _In waiting I waited_
> - <sup>b</sup> 2. Heb. _a pit of noise_
> - <sup>c</sup> 5. Or, _none can order them unto thee_
> - <sup>d</sup> 6. Heb. _digged_
> - <sup>e</sup> 8. Heb. _in the midst of my bowels_
> - <sup>f</sup> 12. Heb. _forsaketh_

## Chapter 41

> _To the chief Musician, A Psalm of David._

<sup>1</sup> Blessed _is_ he that considereth the poor:<sup>a</sup><br>The LORD will deliver him in time of trouble.<sup>b</sup><br>
<sup>2</sup> The LORD will preserve him, and keep him alive; _and_ he shall be blessed upon the earth:<br>And thou wilt not deliver him<sup>c</sup> unto the will of his enemies.<br>
<sup>3</sup> The LORD will strengthen him upon the bed of languishing:<br>Thou wilt make<sup>d</sup> all his bed in his sickness.<br>
<sup>4</sup> I said, LORD, be merciful unto me:<br>Heal my soul; for I have sinned against thee.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Mine enemies speak evil of me,<br>When shall he die, and his name perish?<br>
<sup>6</sup> And if he come to see _me_, he speaketh vanity:<br>His heart gathereth iniquity to itself; _When_ he goeth abroad, he telleth _it_.<br>
<sup>7</sup> All that hate me whisper together against me:<br>Against me do they devise my hurt.<sup>e</sup><br>
<sup>8</sup> An evil disease,<sup>f</sup> _say they_, cleaveth fast unto him:<br>And _now_ that he lieth he shall rise up no more.<br>
<sup>9</sup> Yea, mine own familiar friend,<sup>g</sup> in whom I trusted,<br>Which did eat of my bread, hath lifted up _his_ heel against me.<br>
<sup>10</sup> But thou, O LORD, be merciful unto me,<br>And raise me up, that I may requite them.<br>
<sup>11</sup> By this I know that thou favourest me,<br>Because mine enemy doth not triumph over me.<br>
<sup>12</sup> And as for me, thou upholdest me in mine integrity,<br>And settest me before thy face for ever.<br>
<sup>13</sup> Blessed _be_ the LORD God of Israel<br>From everlasting, and to everlasting. Amen, and Amen.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Or, _the weak_, or _sick_
> - <sup>b</sup> 1. Heb. _in the day of evil_
> - <sup>c</sup> 2. Or, _do not now deliver_
> - <sup>d</sup> 3. Heb. _turn_
> - <sup>e</sup> 7. Heb. _evil to me_
> - <sup>f</sup> 8. Heb. _a thing of Belial_
> - <sup>g</sup> 9. Heb. _the man of my peace_

## Chapter 42

> _To the chief Musician, Maschil, for the sons of Korah._

<sup>1</sup> As the hart panteth<sup>a</sup> after the water brooks,<br>So panteth my soul after thee, O God.<br>
<sup>2</sup> My soul thirsteth for God, for the living God:<br>When shall I come and appear before God?<br>
<sup>3</sup> My tears have been my meat day and night,<br>While they continually say unto me, Where _is_ thy God?<br>
<sup>4</sup> When I remember these _things_, I pour out my soul in me:<br>For I had gone with the multitude, I went with them to the house of God,<br>With the voice of joy and praise,<br>With a multitude that kept holyday.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Why art thou cast down,<sup>b</sup> O my soul?<br>And _why_ art thou disquieted in me?<br>Hope thou in God:<br>For I shall yet praise<sup>c</sup> him _For_ the help of his countenance.<sup>d</sup><br>
<sup>6</sup> O my God, my soul is cast down within me:<br>Therefore will I remember thee from the land of Jordan, and of the Hermonites, from the hill Mizar.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Deep calleth unto deep at the noise of thy waterspouts:<br>All thy waves and thy billows are gone over me.<br>
<sup>8</sup> _Yet_ the LORD will command his lovingkindness in the daytime,<br>And in the night his song _shall be_ with me, _and_ my prayer unto the God of my life.<br>
<sup>9</sup> I will say unto God my rock, why hast thou forgotten me?<br>Why go I mourning because of the oppression of the enemy?<br>
<sup>10</sup> _As_ with a sword<sup>e</sup> in my bones,<br>Mine enemies reproach me;<br>While they say daily unto me,<br>Where _is_ thy God?<br>
<sup>11</sup> Why art thou cast down, O my soul?<br>And why art thou disquieted within me?<br>Hope thou in God;<br>For I shall yet praise him, _who is_ the health of my countenance, and my God.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Heb. _brayeth_
> - <sup>b</sup> 5. Heb. _bowed down_
> - <sup>c</sup> 5. Or, _give thanks_
> - <sup>d</sup> 5. Or, _his presence is salvation_
> - <sup>e</sup> 10. Or, _killing_

## Chapter 43

<sup>1</sup> Judge me, O God, and plead my cause against an ungodly<sup>a</sup> nation:<br>O deliver me from the deceitful and unjust man.<sup>b</sup><br>
<sup>2</sup> For thou _art_ the God of my strength: why dost thou cast me off?<br>Why go I mourning because of the oppression of the enemy?<br>
<sup>3</sup> O send out thy light and thy truth: Let them lead me;<br>Let them bring me unto thy holy hill, and to thy tabernacles.<br>
<sup>4</sup> Then will I go unto the altar of God, unto God my exceeding joy:<sup>c</sup><br>Yea, upon the harp will I praise thee, O God my God.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Why art thou cast down, O my soul?<br>And why art thou disquieted within me?<br>Hope in God:<br>For I shall yet praise him, _Who is_ the health of my countenance, and my God.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Or, _unmerciful_
> - <sup>b</sup> 1. Heb. _from a man of deceit, and iniquity_
> - <sup>c</sup> 4. Heb. _the gladness of my joy_

## Chapter 44

> _To the chief Musician for the sons of Korah, Maschil._

<sup>1</sup> We have heard with our ears, O God, our fathers have told us,<br>_What_ work thou didst in their days, in the times of old.<br>
<sup>2</sup> _How_ thou didst drive out the heathen with thy hand, and plantedst them;<br>_How_ thou didst afflict the people, and cast them out.<br>
<sup>3</sup> For they got not the land in possession by their own sword,<br>Neither did their own arm save them:<br>But thy right hand, and thine arm, and the light of thy countenance,<br>Because thou hadst a favour unto them.<br>
<sup>4</sup> Thou art my King, O God: Command deliverances for Jacob.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Through thee will we push down our enemies:<br>Through thy name will we tread them under that rise up against us.<br>
<sup>6</sup> For I will not trust in my bow,<br>Neither shall my sword save me.<br>
<sup>7</sup> But thou hast saved us from our enemies,<br>And hast put them to shame that hated us.<br>
<sup>8</sup> In God we boast all the day long,<br>And praise thy name for ever. _Selah._<br>
<sup>9</sup> But thou hast cast off, and put us to shame;<br>And goest not forth with our armies.<br>
<sup>10</sup> Thou makest us to turn back from the enemy:<br>And they which hate us spoil for themselves.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Thou hast given us like sheep _appointed_ for meat;<sup>a</sup><br>And hast scattered us among the heathen.<br>
<sup>12</sup> Thou sellest thy people for nought,<sup>b</sup><br>And dost not increase _thy wealth_ by their price.<br>
<sup>13</sup> Thou makest us a reproach to our neighbours,<br>A scorn and a derision to them that are round about us.<br>
<sup>14</sup> Thou makest us a byword among the heathen,<br>A shaking of the head among the people.<br>
<sup>15</sup> My confusion _is_ continually before me,<br>And the shame of my face hath covered me,<br>
<sup>16</sup> For the voice of him that reproacheth and blasphemeth;<br>By reason of the enemy and avenger.<br>
<sup>17</sup> All this is come upon us; yet have we not forgotten thee,<br>Neither have we dealt falsely in thy covenant.<br>
<sup>18</sup> Our heart is not turned back,<br>Neither have our steps<sup>c</sup> declined from thy way;<br>
<sup>19</sup> Though thou hast sore broken us in the place of dragons,<br>And covered us with the shadow of death.<br>
<sup>20</sup> If we have forgotten the name of our God, or stretched out our hands to a strange god;<br>
<sup>21</sup> Shall not God search this out? For he knoweth the secrets of the heart.<br>
<sup>22</sup> Yea, for thy sake are we killed all the day long;<br>We are counted as sheep for the slaughter.<br>
<sup>23</sup> Awake, why sleepest thou, O Lord?<br>Arise, cast _us_ not off for ever.<br>
<sup>24</sup> Wherefore hidest thou thy face,<br>_And_ forgettest our affliction and our oppression?<br>
<sup>25</sup> For our soul is bowed down to the dust:<br>Our belly cleaveth unto the earth.<br>
<sup>26</sup> Arise for our help,<sup>d</sup><br>And redeem us for thy mercies’ sake.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 11. Heb. _as sheep of meat_
> - <sup>b</sup> 12. Heb. _without riches_
> - <sup>c</sup> 18. Or, _goings_
> - <sup>d</sup> 26. Heb. _a help for us_

## Chapter 45

> _To the chief Musician upon Shoshannim, for the sons of Korah, Maschil, A Song of loves._

<sup>1</sup> My heart is inditing<sup>a</sup> a good matter:<br>I speak of the things which I have made touching the king:<br>My tongue _is_<br>The pen of a ready writer.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Thou art fairer than the children of men:<br>Grace is poured into thy lips: Therefore God hath blessed thee for ever.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Gird thy sword upon _thy_ thigh, O _most_ mighty,<br>With thy glory and thy majesty.<br>
<sup>4</sup> And in thy majesty ride prosperously<sup>b</sup><br>Because of truth and meekness _and_ righteousness; And thy right hand shall teach thee terrible things.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Thine arrows _are_ sharp in the heart of the king’s enemies;<br>_Whereby_ the people fall under thee.<br>
<sup>6</sup> Thy throne, O God, _is_ for ever and ever:<br>The sceptre of thy kingdom _is_ a right sceptre.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Thou lovest righteousness, and hatest wickedness:<br>Therefore God, thy God, hath anointed thee with the oil of gladness above thy fellows.<br>
<sup>8</sup> All thy garments _smell_ of myrrh, and aloes, _and_ cassia,<br>Out of the ivory palaces, whereby they have made thee glad.<br>
<sup>9</sup> Kings’ daughters _were_ among thy honourable women:<br>Upon thy right hand did stand the queen in gold of Ophir.<br>
<sup>10</sup> Hearken, O daughter, and consider, and incline thine ear;<br>Forget also thine own people, and thy father’s house;<br>
<sup>11</sup> So shall the king greatly desire thy beauty:<br>For he _is_ thy Lord; and worship thou him.<br>
<sup>12</sup> And the daughter of Tyre _shall be there_ with a gift;<br>_Even_ the rich among the people shall intreat thy favour.<sup>c</sup><br>
<sup>13</sup> The king’s daughter _is_ all glorious within:<br>Her clothing _is_ of wrought gold.<br>
<sup>14</sup> She shall be brought unto the king in raiment of needlework:<br>The virgins her companions that follow her shall be brought unto thee.<br>
<sup>15</sup> With gladness and rejoicing shall they be brought:<br>They shall enter into the king’s palace.<br>
<sup>16</sup> Instead of thy fathers shall be thy children,<br>Whom thou mayest make princes in all the earth.<br>
<sup>17</sup> I will make thy name to be remembered in all generations:<br>Therefore shall the people praise thee for ever and ever.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Heb. _boileth_ or _bublleth up_
> - <sup>b</sup> 4. Heb. _prosper thou, ride thou_
> - <sup>c</sup> 12. Heb. _thy face_

## Chapter 46

> _To the chief Musician for the sons of Korah, A Song upon Alamoth._

<sup>1</sup> God _is_ our refuge and strength,<br>A very present help in trouble.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Therefore will not we fear, though the earth be removed,<br>And though the mountains be carried into the midst of the sea;<sup>a</sup><br>
<sup>3</sup> _Though_ the waters thereof roar _and_ be troubled,<br>_Though_ the mountains shake with the swelling thereof. _Selah._<br>
<sup>4</sup> _There is_ a river, the streams whereof shall make glad the city of God,<br>The holy _place_ of the tabernacles of the most High.<br>
<sup>5</sup> God _is_ in the midst of her; she shall not be moved:<br>God shall help her, _and that_ right early.<sup>b</sup><br>
<sup>6</sup> The heathen raged, the kingdoms were moved:<br>He uttered his voice, the earth melted.<br>
<sup>7</sup> The LORD of hosts _is_ with us;<br>The God of Jacob _is_ our refuge.<sup>c</sup> _Selah._<br>
<sup>8</sup> Come, behold the works of the LORD,<br>What desolations he hath made in the earth.<br>
<sup>9</sup> He maketh wars to cease unto the end of the earth;<br>He breaketh the bow, and cutteth the spear in sunder; He burneth the chariot in the fire.<br>
<sup>10</sup> Be still, and know that I _am_ God:<br>I will be exalted among the heathen, I will be exalted in the earth.<br>
<sup>11</sup> The LORD of hosts _is_ with us;<br>The God of Jacob _is_ our refuge. _Selah._<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 2. Heb. _heart of the seas_
> - <sup>b</sup> 5. Heb. _when the morning appeareth_
> - <sup>c</sup> 7. Heb. _an high place for us_

## Chapter 47

> _To the chief Musician, A Psalm for the sons of Korah._

<sup>1</sup> O clap your hands, all ye people;<br>Shout unto God with the voice of triumph.<br>
<sup>2</sup> For the LORD most high _is_ terrible;<br>_He is_ a great King over all the earth.<br>
<sup>3</sup> He shall subdue the people under us,<br>And the nations under our feet.<br>
<sup>4</sup> He shall choose our inheritance for us,<br>The excellency of Jacob whom he loved. _Selah._<br>
<sup>5</sup> God is gone up with a shout,<br>The LORD with the sound of a trumpet.<br>
<sup>6</sup> Sing praises to God, sing praises:<br>Sing praises unto our King, sing praises.<br>
<sup>7</sup> For God _is_ the King of all the earth:<br>Sing ye praises with understanding.<sup>a</sup><br>
<sup>8</sup> God reigneth over the heathen:<br>God sitteth upon the throne of his holiness.<br>
<sup>9</sup> The princes of the people are gathered together, _even_ the people of the God of Abraham:<br>For the shields of the earth _belong_ unto God: He is greatly exalted.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 7. Or, _every one that hath understanding_

## Chapter 48

> _A Song _and_ Psalm for the sons of Korah._

<sup>1</sup> Great _is_ the LORD, and greatly to be praised<br>In the city of our God, _in_ the mountain of his holiness.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Beautiful for situation, the joy of the whole earth,<br>_Is_ mount Zion, _on_ the sides of the north,<br>The city of the great King.
<sup>3</sup> God is known in her palaces for a refuge.<br>
<sup>4</sup> For, lo, the kings were assembled,<br>They passed by together.<br>
<sup>5</sup> They saw _it, and_ so they marvelled;<br>They were troubled, _and_ hasted away.<br>
<sup>6</sup> Fear took hold upon them there,<br>_And_ pain, as of a woman in travail.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Thou breakest the ships of Tarshish<br>With an east wind.<br>
<sup>8</sup> As we have heard, so have we seen in the city of the LORD of hosts, in the city of our God:<br>God will establish it for ever. _Selah._<br>
<sup>9</sup> We have thought of thy lovingkindness, O God,<br>In the midst of thy temple.<br>
<sup>10</sup> According to thy name, O God, so _is_ thy praise unto the ends of the earth:<br>Thy right hand is full of righteousness.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Let mount Zion rejoice,<br>Let the daughters of Judah be glad, because of thy judgments.<br>
<sup>12</sup> Walk about Zion, and go round about her:<br>Tell the towers thereof.<br>
<sup>13</sup> Mark ye well her bulwarks,<sup>a</sup> consider<sup>b</sup> her palaces;<br>That ye may tell _it_ to the generation following.<br>
<sup>14</sup> For this God _is_ our God for ever and ever:<br>He will be our guide _even_ unto death.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 13. Heb. _Set your heart to her bulwarks_
> - <sup>b</sup> 13. Or, _raise up_

## Chapter 49

> _To the chief Musician, A Psalm for the sons of Korah._

<sup>1</sup> Hear this, all _ye_ people;<br>Give ear, all _ye_ inhabitants of the world:<br>
<sup>2</sup> Both low and high,<br>Rich and poor, together.<br>
<sup>3</sup> My mouth shall speak of wisdom;<br>And the meditation of my heart _shall be_ of understanding.<br>
<sup>4</sup> I will incline mine ear to a parable:<br>I will open my dark saying upon the harp.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Wherefore should I fear in the days of evil,<br>_When_ the iniquity of my heels shall compass me about?<br>
<sup>6</sup> They that trust in their wealth,<br>And boast themselves in the multitude of their riches;<br>
<sup>7</sup> None _of them_ can by any means redeem his brother,<br>Nor give to God a ransom for him:<br>
<sup>8</sup> (For the redemption of their soul _is_ precious,<br>And it ceaseth for ever:)<br>
<sup>9</sup> That he should still live for ever,<br>_And_ not see corruption.<br>
<sup>10</sup> For he seeth _that_ wise men die,<br>Likewise the fool and the brutish person perish, and leave their wealth to others.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Their inward thought _is, that_ their houses _shall continue_ for ever,<br>_And_ their dwelling places to all generations;<sup>a</sup><br>They call _their_ lands after their own names.<br>
<sup>12</sup> Nevertheless man _being_ in honour abideth not:<br>He is like the beasts _that_ perish.<br>
<sup>13</sup> This their way _is_ their folly:<br>Yet their posterity approve their sayings.<sup>b</sup> _Selah._<br>
<sup>14</sup> Like sheep they are laid in the grave; Death shall feed on them; And the upright shall have dominion over them in the morning;<br>And their beauty<sup>c</sup> shall consume in the grave from their dwelling.<br>
<sup>15</sup> But God will redeem my soul from the power<sup>d</sup> of the grave:<sup>e</sup><br>For he shall receive me. _Selah._<br>
<sup>16</sup> Be not thou afraid when one is made rich,<br>When the glory of his house is increased;<br>
<sup>17</sup> For when he dieth he shall carry nothing away:<br>His glory shall not descend after him.<br>
<sup>18</sup> Though while he lived he blessed his soul,<br>And _men_ will praise thee, when thou doest well to thyself.<br>
<sup>19</sup> He shall go<sup>f</sup> to the generation of his fathers;<br>They shall never see light.<br>
<sup>20</sup> Man _that is_ in honour, and understandeth not,<br>Is like the beasts _that_ perish.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 11. Heb. _to generation and generation_
> - <sup>b</sup> 13. Heb. _delight in their mouth_
> - <sup>c</sup> 14. Or, _strength_
> - <sup>d</sup> 15. Heb. _from the hand of the grave_
> - <sup>e</sup> 15. Or, _hell_
> - <sup>f</sup> 19. Heb. _the soul shall go_

## Chapter 50

> _A Psalm of Asaph._

<sup>1</sup> The mighty God, _even_ the LORD, hath spoken,<br>And called the earth from the rising of the sun unto the going down thereof.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Out of Zion, the perfection of beauty,<br>God hath shined.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Our God shall come, and shall not keep silence:<br>A fire shall devour before him, and it shall be very tempestuous round about him.<br>
<sup>4</sup> He shall call to the heavens from above,<br>And to the earth, that he may judge his people.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Gather my saints together unto me;<br>Those that have made a covenant with me by sacrifice.<br>
<sup>6</sup> And the heavens shall declare his righteousness:<br>For God _is_ judge himself. _Selah._<br>
<sup>7</sup> Hear, O my people, and I will speak;<br>O Israel, and I will testify against thee: I _am_ God, _even_ thy God.<br>
<sup>8</sup> I will not reprove thee for thy sacrifices<br>Or thy burnt offerings, _to have been_ continually before me.<br>
<sup>9</sup> I will take no bullock out of thy house,<br>_Nor_ he goats out of thy folds.<br>
<sup>10</sup> For every beast of the forest _is_ mine,<br>_And_ the cattle upon a thousand hills.<br>
<sup>11</sup> I know all the fowls of the mountains:<br>And the wild beasts of the field _are_ mine.<sup>a</sup><br>
<sup>12</sup> If I were hungry, I would not tell thee:<br>For the world _is_ mine, and the fulness thereof.<br>
<sup>13</sup> Will I eat the flesh of bulls,<br>Or drink the blood of goats?<br>
<sup>14</sup> Offer unto God thanksgiving;<br>And pay thy vows unto the most High:<br>
<sup>15</sup> And call upon me in the day of trouble:<br>I will deliver thee, and thou shalt glorify me.<br>
<sup>16</sup> But unto the wicked God saith,<br>What hast thou to do to declare my statutes, or _that_ thou shouldest take my covenant in thy mouth?<br>
<sup>17</sup> Seeing thou hatest instruction,<br>And castest my words behind thee.<br>
<sup>18</sup> When thou sawest a thief, then thou consentedst with him,<br>And hast been partaker with adulterers.<sup>b</sup><br>
<sup>19</sup> Thou givest<sup>c</sup> thy mouth to evil,<br>And thy tongue frameth deceit.<br>
<sup>20</sup> Thou sittest _and_ speakest against thy brother;<br>Thou slanderest thine own mother’s son.<br>
<sup>21</sup> These _things_ hast thou done, and I kept silence;<br>Thou thoughtest that I was altogether _such an one_ as thyself:<br>_But_ I will reprove thee, and set _them_ in order before thine eyes.<br>
<sup>22</sup> Now consider this, ye that forget God,<br>Lest I tear _you_ in pieces, and _there be_ none to deliver.<br>
<sup>23</sup> Whoso offereth praise glorifieth me:<br>And to him that ordereth _his_ conversation<sup>d</sup> _aright_ will I shew the salvation of God.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 11. Heb. _with me_
> - <sup>b</sup> 18. Heb. _thy portion was with adulterers_
> - <sup>c</sup> 19. Heb. _thou sendest_
> - <sup>d</sup> 23. Heb. _that disposeth his way_

## Chapter 51

> _To the chief Musician, A Psalm of David, when Nathan the prophet came unto him, after he had gone in to Bath-sheba._

<sup>1</sup> Have mercy upon me, O God, according to thy lovingkindness:<br>According unto the multitude of thy tender mercies blot out my transgressions.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Wash me throughly from mine iniquity,<br>And cleanse me from my sin.<br>
<sup>3</sup> For I acknowledge my transgressions:<br>And my sin _is_ ever before me.<br>
<sup>4</sup> Against thee, thee only, have I sinned, and done _this_ evil in thy sight:<br>That thou mightest be justified when thou speakest, _and_ be clear when thou judgest.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Behold, I was shapen in iniquity;<br>And in sin did my mother conceive me.<sup>a</sup><br>
<sup>6</sup> Behold, thou desirest truth in the inward parts:<br>And in the hidden _part_ thou shalt make me to know wisdom.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Purge me with hyssop, and I shall be clean:<br>Wash me, and I shall be whiter than snow.<br>
<sup>8</sup> Make me to hear joy and gladness;<br>_That_ the bones _which_ thou hast broken may rejoice.<br>
<sup>9</sup> Hide thy face from my sins,<br>And blot out all mine iniquities.<br>
<sup>10</sup> Create in me a clean heart, O God;<br>And renew a right spirit<sup>b</sup> within me.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Cast me not away from thy presence;<br>And take not thy holy spirit from me.<br>
<sup>12</sup> Restore unto me the joy of thy salvation;<br>And uphold me _with thy_ free spirit.<br>
<sup>13</sup> _Then_ will I teach transgressors thy ways;<br>And sinners shall be converted unto thee.<br>
<sup>14</sup> Deliver me from bloodguiltiness,<sup>c</sup> O God, thou God of my salvation:<br>_And_ my tongue shall sing aloud of thy righteousness.<br>
<sup>15</sup> O Lord, open thou my lips;<br>And my mouth shall shew forth thy praise.<br>
<sup>16</sup> For thou desirest not sacrifice; else would I give _it:_<sup>d</sup><br>Thou delightest not in burnt offering.<br>
<sup>17</sup> The sacrifices of God _are_ a broken spirit:<br>A broken and a contrite heart, O God, thou wilt not despise.<br>
<sup>18</sup> Do good in thy good pleasure unto Zion:<br>Build thou the walls of Jerusalem.<br>
<sup>19</sup> Then shalt thou be pleased with the sacrifices of righteousness, with burnt offering and whole burnt offering:<br>Then shall they offer bullocks upon thine altar.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 5. Heb. _warm me_
> - <sup>b</sup> 10. Or, _a constant spirit_
> - <sup>c</sup> 14. Heb. _bloods_
> - <sup>d</sup> 16. Or, _that I should give it_

## Chapter 52

> _To the chief Musician, Maschil, _A Psalm_ of David, when Doeg the Edomite came and told Saul, and said unto him, David is come to the house of Ahimelech._

<sup>1</sup> Why boastest thou thyself in mischief, O mighty man?<br>The goodness of God _endureth_ continually.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Thy tongue deviseth mischiefs;<br>Like a sharp razor, working deceitfully.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Thou lovest evil more than good;<br>_And_ lying rather than to speak righteousness. _Selah._<br>
<sup>4</sup> Thou lovest all devouring words,<br>O _thou_ deceitful tongue.<sup>a</sup><br>
<sup>5</sup> God shall likewise destroy thee<sup>b</sup> for ever,<br>He shall take thee away, and pluck thee out of _thy_ dwelling place, and root thee out of the land of the living. _Selah._<br>
<sup>6</sup> The righteous also shall see, and fear,<br>And shall laugh at him:<br>
<sup>7</sup> Lo, _this is_ the man _that_ made not God his strength;<br>But trusted in the abundance of his riches, _and_ strengthened himself in his wickedness.<sup>c</sup><br>
<sup>8</sup> But I _am_ like a green olive tree in the house of God:<br>I trust in the mercy of God for ever and ever.<br>
<sup>9</sup> I will praise thee for ever, because thou hast done _it:_<br>And I will wait on thy name; for _it is_ good before thy saints.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 4. Or, _and the deceitful tongue_
> - <sup>b</sup> 5. Heb. _beat thee down_
> - <sup>c</sup> 7. Or, _substance_

## Chapter 53

> _To the chief Musician upon Mahalath, Maschil, _A Psalm_ of David._

<sup>1</sup> The fool hath said in his heart,<br>_There is_ no God.<br>Corrupt are they, and have done abominable iniquity:<br>_There is_ none that doeth good.<br>
<sup>2</sup> God looked down from heaven upon the children of men,<br>To see if there were _any_ that did understand, that did seek God.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Every one of them is gone back: they are altogether become filthy;<br>_There is_ none that doeth good, no, not one.<br>
<sup>4</sup> Have the workers of iniquity no knowledge?<br>Who eat up my people _as_ they eat bread: They have not called upon God.<br>
<sup>5</sup> There were they in great fear,<sup>a</sup> _where_ no fear was:<br>For God hath scattered the bones of him that encampeth _against_ thee:<br>Thou hast put _them_ to shame,<br>because God hath despised them.<br>
<sup>6</sup> Oh that the salvation<sup>b</sup> of Israel _were come_ out of Zion!<br>When God bringeth back the captivity of his people,<br>Jacob shall rejoice,<br>_And_ Israel shall be glad.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 5. Heb. _they feared a fear_
> - <sup>b</sup> 6. Heb. _Who will give salvation_, etc.

## Chapter 54

> _To the chief Musician on Neginoth, Maschil, _A Psalm_ of David, when the Ziphim came and said to Saul, Doth not David hide himself with us?_

<sup>1</sup> Save me, O God, by thy name,<br>And judge me by thy strength.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Hear my prayer, O God;<br>Give ear to the words of my mouth.<br>
<sup>3</sup> For strangers are risen up against me,<br>And oppressors seek after my soul: They have not set God before them. _Selah._<br>
<sup>4</sup> Behold, God _is_ mine helper:<br>The Lord _is_ with them that uphold my soul.<br>
<sup>5</sup> He shall reward evil unto mine enemies:<sup>a</sup><br>Cut them off in thy truth.<br>
<sup>6</sup> I will freely sacrifice unto thee:<br>I will praise thy name, O LORD; for _it is_ good.<br>
<sup>7</sup> For he hath delivered me out of all trouble:<br>And mine eye hath seen _his desire_ upon mine enemies.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 5. Heb. _those that observe me_

## Chapter 55

> _To the chief Musician on Neginoth, Maschil, _A Psalm_ of David._

<sup>1</sup> Give ear to my prayer, O God;<br>And hide not thyself from my supplication.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Attend unto me, and hear me:<br>I mourn in my complaint, and make a noise;<br>
<sup>3</sup> Because of the voice of the enemy,<br>Because of the oppression of the wicked:<br>For they cast iniquity upon me,<br>And in wrath they hate me.<br>
<sup>4</sup> My heart is sore pained within me:<br>And the terrors of death are fallen upon me.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Fearfulness and trembling are come upon me,<br>And horror hath overwhelmed me.<sup>a</sup><br>
<sup>6</sup> And I said, Oh that I had wings like a dove!<br>_For then_ would I fly away, and be at rest.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Lo, _then_ would I wander far off,<br>_And_ remain in the wilderness. _Selah._<br>
<sup>8</sup> I would hasten my escape<br>From the windy storm _and_ tempest.<br>
<sup>9</sup> Destroy, O Lord, _and_ divide their tongues:<br>For I have seen violence and strife in the city.<br>
<sup>10</sup> Day and night they go about it upon the walls thereof:<br>Mischief also and sorrow _are_ in the midst of it.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Wickedness _is_ in the midst thereof:<br>Deceit and guile depart not from her streets.<br>
<sup>12</sup> For _it was_ not an enemy _that_ reproached me;<br>Then I could have born _it:_<br>Neither _was it_ he that hated me _that_ did magnify _himself_ against me;<br>Then I would have hid myself from him:<br>
<sup>13</sup> But _it was_ thou, a man mine equal,<br>My guide, and mine acquaintance.<br>
<sup>14</sup> We took sweet counsel<sup>b</sup> together,<br>_And_ walked unto the house of God in company.<br>
<sup>15</sup> Let death seize upon them, _and_ let them go down quick into hell:<sup>c</sup><br>For wickedness _is_ in their dwellings, _and_ among them.<br>
<sup>16</sup> As for me, I will call upon God;<br>And the LORD shall save me.<br>
<sup>17</sup> Evening, and morning, and at noon, will I pray, and cry aloud:<br>And he shall hear my voice.<br>
<sup>18</sup> He hath delivered my soul in peace from the battle _that was_ against me:<br>For there were many with me.<br>
<sup>19</sup> God shall hear, and afflict them, even he that abideth of old. _Selah._<br>Because they have no changes, therefore they fear not God.<sup>d</sup><br>
<sup>20</sup> He hath put forth his hands against such as be at peace with him:<br>He hath broken<sup>e</sup> his covenant.<br>
<sup>21</sup> _The words_ of his mouth were smoother than butter, but war _was_ in his heart:<br>His words were softer than oil, yet _were_ they drawn swords.<br>
<sup>22</sup> Cast thy burden<sup>f</sup> upon the LORD, and he shall sustain thee:<br>He shall never suffer the righteous to be moved.<br>
<sup>23</sup> But thou,<br>O God, shalt bring them down into the pit of destruction:<br>Bloody and deceitful men<sup>g</sup> shall not live out half their days;<sup>h</sup><br>But I will trust in thee.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 5. Heb. _covered me_
> - <sup>b</sup> 14. Heb. _who sweetened counsel_
> - <sup>c</sup> 15. Or, _the grave_
> - <sup>d</sup> 19. Or, _with whom also there be no changes, yet they fear not God_
> - <sup>e</sup> 20. Heb. _he hath profaned_
> - <sup>f</sup> 22. Or, _gift_
> - <sup>g</sup> 23. Heb. _men of bloods and deceit_
> - <sup>h</sup> 23. Heb. _shall not half their days_

## Chapter 56

> _To the chief Musician upon Jonath-elem-rechokim, Michtam of David, when the Philistines took him in Gath._

<sup>1</sup> Be merciful unto me, O God: for man would swallow me up;<br>He fighting daily oppresseth me.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Mine enemies<sup>a</sup> would daily swallow _me_ up:<br>For _they be_ many that fight against me, O thou most High.<br>
<sup>3</sup> What time I am afraid,<br>I will trust in thee.<br>
<sup>4</sup> In God I will praise his word,<br>In God I have put my trust; I will not fear what flesh can do unto me.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Every day they wrest my words:<br>All their thoughts _are_ against me for evil.<br>
<sup>6</sup> They gather themselves together, they hide themselves,<br>They mark my steps, when they wait for my soul.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Shall they escape by iniquity?<br>In _thine_ anger cast down the people, O God.<br>
<sup>8</sup> Thou tellest my wanderings: Put thou my tears into thy bottle:<br>_Are they_ not in thy book?<br>
<sup>9</sup> When I cry _unto thee_, then shall mine enemies turn back:<br>This I know; for God _is_ for me.<br>
<sup>10</sup> In God will I praise _his_ word:<br>In the LORD will I praise _his_ word.<br>
<sup>11</sup> In God have I put my trust:<br>I will not be afraid what man can do unto me.<br>
<sup>12</sup> Thy vows _are_ upon me, O God:<br>I will render praises unto thee.<br>
<sup>13</sup> For thou hast delivered my soul from death: _wilt_ not _thou deliver_ my feet from falling,<br>That I may walk before God in the light of the living?<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 2. Heb. _mine observers_

## Chapter 57

> _To the chief Musician, Altaschith, Michtam of David, when he fled from Saul in the cave._

<sup>1</sup> Be merciful unto me, O God, be merciful unto me: For my soul trusteth in thee:<br>Yea, in the shadow of thy wings will I make my refuge, until _these_ calamities be overpast.<br>
<sup>2</sup> I will cry unto God most high;<br>Unto God that performeth _all things_ for me.<br>
<sup>3</sup> He shall send from heaven, and save me<br>_From_ the reproach of him that would swallow me up. _Selah._<br>God shall send forth his mercy and his truth.<br>
<sup>4</sup> My soul _is_ among lions:<br>_And_ I lie _even among_ them that are set on fire,<br>_Even_ the sons of men, whose teeth _are_ spears and arrows, and their tongue a sharp sword.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Be thou exalted, O God, above the heavens;<br>_Let_ thy glory _be_ above all the earth.<br>
<sup>6</sup> They have prepared a net for my steps; My soul is bowed down:<br>They have digged a pit before me, into the midst whereof they are fallen _themselves_. _Selah._<br>
<sup>7</sup> My heart is fixed,<sup>a</sup> O God, my heart is fixed:<br>I will sing and give praise.<br>
<sup>8</sup> Awake up, my glory; awake, psaltery and harp:<br>I _myself_ will awake early.<br>
<sup>9</sup> I will praise thee, O Lord, among the people:<br>I will sing unto thee among the nations.<br>
<sup>10</sup> For thy mercy _is_ great unto the heavens,<br>And thy truth unto the clouds.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Be thou exalted, O God, above the heavens:<br>_Let_ thy glory _be_ above all the earth.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 7. Or, _prepared_

## Chapter 58

> _To the chief Musician, Al-taschith, Michtam of David._

<sup>1</sup> Do ye indeed speak righteousness, O congregation?<br>Do ye judge uprightly, O ye sons of men?<br>
<sup>2</sup> Yea, in heart ye work wickedness;<br>Ye weigh the violence of your hands in the earth.<br>
<sup>3</sup> The wicked are estranged from the womb:<br>They go astray as soon as they be born,<sup>a</sup> speaking lies.<br>
<sup>4</sup> Their poison _is_ like the<sup>b</sup> poison of a serpent:<br>_They are_ like the deaf adder<sup>c</sup> _that_ stoppeth her ear;<br>
<sup>5</sup> Which will not hearken to the voice of charmers,<br>Charming never so wisely.<sup>d</sup><br>
<sup>6</sup> Break their teeth, O God, in their mouth: Break out the great teeth of the young lions, O LORD.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Let them melt away as waters _which_ run continually: _when_ he bendeth _his bow to shoot_ his arrows, let them be as cut in pieces.<br>
<sup>8</sup> As a snail _which_ melteth, let _every one of them_ pass away: _Like_ the untimely birth of a woman,<br>_That_ they may not see the sun.<br>
<sup>9</sup> Before your pots can feel the thorns,<br>He shall take them away as with a whirlwind, both living, and in _his_ wrath.<sup>e</sup><br>
<sup>10</sup> The righteous shall rejoice when he seeth the vengeance:<br>He shall wash his feet in the blood of the wicked.<br>
<sup>11</sup> So that a man shall say, Verily _there is_ a reward for<sup>f</sup> the righteous:<br>Verily he is a God that judgeth in the earth.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 3. Heb. _from the belly_
> - <sup>b</sup> 4. Heb. _according to the likeness_
> - <sup>c</sup> 4. Or, _asp_
> - <sup>d</sup> 5. Or, _be the charmer never so cunning_
> - <sup>e</sup> 9. Heb. _as living, as wrath_
> - <sup>f</sup> 11. Heb. _fruit of the_ etc.

## Chapter 59

> _To the chief Musician, Al-taschith, Michtam of David; when Saul sent, and they watched the house to kill him._

<sup>1</sup> Deliver me from mine enemies, O my God:<br>Defend me<sup>a</sup> from them that rise up against me.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Deliver me from the workers of iniquity,<br>And save me from bloody men.<br>
<sup>3</sup> For, lo, they lie in wait for my soul:<br>The mighty are gathered against me; Not _for_ my transgression, nor _for_ my sin, O LORD.<br>
<sup>4</sup> They run and prepare themselves without _my_ fault.<br>Awake to help me,<sup>b</sup> and behold.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Thou therefore, O LORD God of hosts, the God of Israel,<br>Awake to visit all the heathen: Be not merciful to any wicked transgressors. _Selah._<br>
<sup>6</sup> They return at evening:<br>They make a noise like a dog, and go round about the city.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Behold, they belch out with their mouth: Swords _are_ in their lips:<br>For who, _say they_, doth hear?<br>
<sup>8</sup> But thou, O LORD, shalt laugh at them;<br>Thou shalt have all the heathen in derision.<br>
<sup>9</sup> _Because of_ his strength will I wait upon thee:<br>For God _is_ my defence.<sup>c</sup><br>
<sup>10</sup> The God of my mercy shall prevent me:<br>God shall let me see _my desire_ upon mine enemies.<sup>d</sup><br>
<sup>11</sup> Slay them not, lest my people forget:<br>Scatter them by thy power; and bring them down, O Lord our shield.<br>
<sup>12</sup> _For_ the sin of their mouth _and_ the words of their lips let them even be taken in their pride:<br>And for cursing and lying _which_ they speak.<br>
<sup>13</sup> Consume _them_ in wrath, consume _them_, that they _may_ not _be:_<br>And let them know that God ruleth in Jacob unto the ends of the earth. _Selah._<br>
<sup>14</sup> And at evening let them return;<br>_And_ let them make a noise like a dog, and go round about the city.<br>
<sup>15</sup> Let them wander up and down for meat,<sup>e</sup><br>And grudge if they be not satisfied.<sup>f</sup><br>
<sup>16</sup> But I will sing of thy power; Yea, I will sing aloud of thy mercy in the morning:<br>For thou hast been my defence and refuge in the day of my trouble.<br>
<sup>17</sup> Unto thee, O my strength, will I sing:<br>For God _is_ my defence, _and_ the God of my mercy.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Heb. _set me on high_
> - <sup>b</sup> 4. Heb. _to meet me_
> - <sup>c</sup> 9. Heb. _my high place_
> - <sup>d</sup> 10. Heb. _mine observers_
> - <sup>e</sup> 15. Heb. _to eat_
> - <sup>f</sup> 15. Or, _if they be not satisfied, then they will stay all night_

## Chapter 60

> _To the chief Musician upon Shushan-eduth, Michtam of David, to teach; when he strove with Aram-naharaim and with Aram-zobah, when Joab returned, and smote of Edom in the valley of salt twelve thousand._

<sup>1</sup> O God, thou hast cast us off, thou has scattered<sup>a</sup> us,<br>Thou hast been displeased; O turn thyself to us again.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Thou hast made the earth to tremble; thou hast broken it:<br>Heal the breaches thereof; for it shaketh.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Thou hast shewed thy people hard things:<br>Thou hast made us to drink the wine of astonishment.<br>
<sup>4</sup> Thou hast given a banner to them that fear thee,<br>That it may be displayed because of the truth. _Selah._<br>
<sup>5</sup> That thy beloved may be delivered;<br>Save _with_ thy right hand, and hear me.<br>
<sup>6</sup> God hath spoken in his holiness; I will rejoice, I will divide Shechem,<br>And mete out the valley of Succoth.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Gilead _is_ mine, and Manasseh _is_ mine;<br>Ephraim also _is_ the strength of mine head; Judah _is_ my lawgiver;<br>
<sup>8</sup> Moab _is_ my washpot; Over Edom will I cast out my shoe:<br>Philistia, triumph thou because of me.<sup>b</sup><br>
<sup>9</sup> Who will bring me _into_ the strong city?<sup>c</sup><br>Who will lead me into Edom?<br>
<sup>10</sup> _Wilt_ not thou, O God, _which_ hadst cast us off?<br>And _thou_, O God, _which_ didst not go out with our armies?<br>
<sup>11</sup> Give us help from trouble:<br>For vain _is_ the help of man.<sup>d</sup><br>
<sup>12</sup> Through God we shall do valiantly:<br>For he _it is that_ shall tread down our enemies.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Heb. _broken_
> - <sup>b</sup> 8. Or, _triumph thou over me: (by an irony)_
> - <sup>c</sup> 9. Heb. _city of strength_
> - <sup>d</sup> 11. Heb. _salvation_

## Chapter 61

> _To the chief Musician upon Neginah, _A Psalm_ of David._

<sup>1</sup> Hear my cry, O God;<br>Attend unto my prayer.<br>
<sup>2</sup> From the end of the earth will I cry unto thee,<br>When my heart is overwhelmed:<br>Lead me to the rock _that_ is higher than I.<br>
<sup>3</sup> For thou hast been a shelter for me, _and_ a strong tower from the enemy.<br>
<sup>4</sup> I will abide in thy tabernacle for ever:<br>I will trust<sup>a</sup> in the covert of thy wings. _Selah._<br>
<sup>5</sup> For thou, O God, hast heard my vows:<br>Thou hast given _me_ the heritage of those that fear thy name.<br>
<sup>6</sup> Thou wilt prolong the king’s life:<br>_And_ his years as many generations.<sup>b</sup><br>
<sup>7</sup> He shall abide before God for ever:<br>O prepare mercy and truth, _which_ may preserve him.<br>
<sup>8</sup> So will I sing praise unto thy name for ever,<br>That I may daily perform my vows.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 4. Or, _make my refuge_
> - <sup>b</sup> 6. Heb. _as generation and generation_

## Chapter 62

> _To the chief Musician, to Jeduthun, A Psalm of David._

<sup>1</sup> Truly<sup>a</sup> my soul waiteth<sup>b</sup> upon God:<br>From him _cometh_ my salvation.<br>
<sup>2</sup> He only _is_ my rock and my salvation;<br>_He is_ my defence;<sup>c</sup> I shall not be greatly moved.<br>
<sup>3</sup> How long will ye imagine mischief against a man?<br>Ye shall be slain all of you: As a bowing wall _shall ye be, and as_ a tottering fence.<br>
<sup>4</sup> They only consult to cast _him_ down from his excellency:<br>They delight in lies: They bless with their mouth, but they curse inwardly.<sup>d</sup> _Selah._<br>
<sup>5</sup> My soul, wait thou only upon God;<br>For my expectation _is_ from him.<br>
<sup>6</sup> He only _is_ my rock and my salvation:<br>_He is_ my defence; I shall not be moved.<br>
<sup>7</sup> In God _is_ my salvation and my glory:<br>The rock of my strength, _and_ my refuge, _is_ in God.<br>
<sup>8</sup> Trust in him at all times; ye people,<br>Pour out your heart before him: God _is_ a refuge for us. _Selah._<br>
<sup>9</sup> Surely men of low degree _are_ vanity,<br>_and_ men of high degree _are_ a lie: To be laid in the balance, they _are_ altogether<sup>e</sup> _lighter_ than vanity.<br>
<sup>10</sup> Trust not in oppression, and become not vain in robbery:<br>If riches increase, set not your heart _upon them_.<br>
<sup>11</sup> God hath spoken once; Twice have I heard this;<br>That power<sup>f</sup> _belongeth_ unto God.<br>
<sup>12</sup> Also unto thee, O Lord, _belongeth_ mercy:<br>For thou renderest to every man according to his work.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Or, _only_
> - <sup>b</sup> 1. Heb. _is silent_
> - <sup>c</sup> 2. Heb. _high place_
> - <sup>d</sup> 4. Heb. _in their inward parts_
> - <sup>e</sup> 9. Or, _alike_
> - <sup>f</sup> 11. Or, _strength_

## Chapter 63

> _A Psalm of David, when he was in the wilderness of Judah._

<sup>1</sup> O God, thou _art_<br>My God; early will I seek thee:<br>My soul thirsteth for thee, my flesh longeth for thee<br>In a dry and thirsty<sup>a</sup> land, where no water is;<sup>b</sup><br>
<sup>2</sup> To see thy power and thy glory,<br>So _as_ I have seen thee in the sanctuary.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Because thy lovingkindness<br>_Is_ better than life,<br>My lips shall praise thee.<br>
<sup>4</sup> Thus will I bless thee while I live: I will lift up my hands in thy name.<br>
<sup>5</sup> My soul shall be satisfied as _with_ marrow<sup>c</sup> and fatness;<br>And my mouth shall praise _thee_ with joyful lips:<br>
<sup>6</sup> When I remember thee upon my bed,<br>_And_ meditate on thee in the _night_ watches.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Because thou hast been my help,<br>Therefore in the shadow of thy wings will I rejoice.<br>
<sup>8</sup> My soul followeth hard after thee:<br>Thy right hand upholdeth me.<br>
<sup>9</sup> But those _that_ seek my soul, to destroy _it_,<br>Shall go into the lower parts of the earth.<br>
<sup>10</sup> They shall fall by the sword:<sup>d</sup><br>They shall be a portion for foxes.<br>
<sup>11</sup> But the king shall rejoice in God; Every one that sweareth by him shall glory:<br>But the mouth of them that speak lies shall be stopped.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Heb. _weary_
> - <sup>b</sup> 1. Heb. _without water_
> - <sup>c</sup> 5. Heb. _fatness_
> - <sup>d</sup> 10. Heb. _They shall make him run out like water by the hands of the sword_

## Chapter 64

> _To the chief Musician, A Psalm of David._

<sup>1</sup> Hear my voice, O God, in my prayer:<br>Preserve my life from fear of the enemy.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Hide me from the secret counsel of the wicked;<br>From the insurrection of the workers of iniquity:<br>
<sup>3</sup> Who whet their tongue like a sword,<br>_And_ bend _their bows to shoot_ their arrows, _even_ bitter words:<br>
<sup>4</sup> That they may shoot in secret at the perfect:<br>Suddenly do they shoot at him, and fear not.<br>
<sup>5</sup> They encourage themselves _in_ an evil matter:<sup>a</sup><br>They commune of laying snares<sup>b</sup> privily; They say, Who shall see them?<br>
<sup>6</sup> They search out iniquities; they accomplish a diligent search:<sup>c</sup><br>Both the inward _thought_ of every one _of them_, and the heart, _is_ deep.<br>
<sup>7</sup> But God shall shoot at them<br>_With_ an arrow; suddenly shall they be wounded.<sup>d</sup><br>
<sup>8</sup> So they shall make their own tongue to fall upon themselves:<br>All that see them shall flee away.<br>
<sup>9</sup> And all men shall fear, and shall declare the work of God;<br>For they shall wisely consider of his doing.<br>
<sup>10</sup> The righteous shall be glad in the LORD, and shall trust in him;<br>And all the upright in heart shall glory.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 5. Or, _speech_
> - <sup>b</sup> 5. Heb. _to hide snares_
> - <sup>c</sup> 6. Heb. _a search, searched_
> - <sup>d</sup> 7. Heb. _their wound shall be_

## Chapter 65

> _To the chief Musician, A Psalm _and_ Song of David._

<sup>1</sup> Praise waiteth<sup>a</sup> for thee, O God, in Zion:<br>And unto thee shall the vow be performed.<br>
<sup>2</sup> O thou that hearest prayer,<br>Unto thee shall all flesh come.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Iniquities prevail<sup>b</sup> against me:<br>_As for_ our transgressions, thou shalt purge them away.<br>
<sup>4</sup> Blessed _is the man whom_ thou choosest, and causest to approach _unto thee_,<br>_That_ he may dwell in thy courts: We shall be satisfied with the goodness of thy house, _even_ of thy holy temple.<br>
<sup>5</sup> _By_ terrible things in righteousness wilt thou answer us, O God of our salvation;<br>_Who art_ the confidence of all the ends of the earth, and of them that are afar off _upon_ the sea:<br>
<sup>6</sup> Which by his strength setteth fast the mountains;<br>_Being_ girded with power:<br>
<sup>7</sup> Which stilleth the noise of the seas,<br>the noise of their waves, and the tumult of the people.<br>
<sup>8</sup> They also that dwell in the uttermost parts are afraid at thy tokens:<br>Thou makest the outgoings of the morning and evening to rejoice.<sup>c</sup><br>
<sup>9</sup> Thou visitest the earth, and waterest it:<sup>d</sup><br>Thou greatly enrichest it<br>With the river of God, _which_ is full of water:<br>Thou preparest them corn, when thou hast so provided for it.<br>
<sup>10</sup> Thou waterest the ridges thereof abundantly: Thou settlest<sup>e</sup> the furrows thereof:<br>Thou makest it soft<sup>f</sup> with showers: Thou blessest the springing thereof.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Thou crownest the year with thy goodness;<sup>g</sup><br>And thy paths drop fatness.<br>
<sup>12</sup> They drop _upon_ the pastures of the wilderness:<br>And the little hills rejoice on every side.<sup>h</sup><br>
<sup>13</sup> The pastures are clothed with flocks;<br>The valleys also are covered over with corn; They shout for joy, they also sing.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Heb. _is silent_
> - <sup>b</sup> 3. Heb. _words_ or _matters of iniquities_
> - <sup>c</sup> 8. Or, _to sing_
> - <sup>d</sup> 9. Or, _after thou hadst made it to desire rain_
> - <sup>e</sup> 10. Or, _thou causest rain to descend into the furrows thereof_
> - <sup>f</sup> 10. Heb. _thou dissolvest it_
> - <sup>g</sup> 11. Heb. _the year of thy goodness_
> - <sup>h</sup> 12. Heb. _are girded with joy_

## Chapter 66

> _To the chief Musician, A Song _or_ Psalm._

<sup>1</sup> Make a joyful noise unto God, all ye lands:<sup>a</sup><br>
<sup>2</sup> Sing forth the honour of his name: Make his praise glorious.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Say unto God, How terrible _art thou in_ thy works!<br>Through the greatness of thy power shall thine enemies submit themselves<sup>b</sup> unto thee.<br>
<sup>4</sup> All the earth shall worship thee,<br>And shall sing unto thee; They shall sing _to_ thy name. _Selah._<br>
<sup>5</sup> Come and see the works of God:<br>_He is_ terrible _in his_ doing toward the children of men.<br>
<sup>6</sup> He turned the sea into dry _land:_<br>They went through the flood on foot: There did we rejoice in him.<br>
<sup>7</sup> He ruleth by his power for ever; His eyes behold the nations:<br>Let not the rebellious exalt themselves. _Selah._<br>
<sup>8</sup> O bless our God, ye people,<br>And make the voice of his praise to be heard:<br>
<sup>9</sup> Which holdeth<sup>c</sup> our soul in life,<br>And suffereth not our feet to be moved.<br>
<sup>10</sup> For thou, O God, hast proved us:<br>Thou hast tried us, as silver is tried.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Thou broughtest us into the net;<br>Thou laidst affliction upon our loins.<br>
<sup>12</sup> Thou hast caused men to ride over our heads;<br>We went through fire and through water: But thou broughtest us out into a wealthy<sup>d</sup> _place_.<br>
<sup>13</sup> I will go into thy house with burnt offerings:<br>I will pay thee my vows,
<sup>14</sup> which my lips have uttered,<sup>e</sup> and my mouth hath spoken, when I was in trouble.<br>
<sup>15</sup> I will offer unto thee burnt sacrifices of fatlings,<sup>f</sup> with the incense of rams;<br>I will offer bullocks with goats. _Selah._<br>
<sup>16</sup> Come _and_ hear, all ye that fear God,<br>And I will declare what he hath done for my soul.<br>
<sup>17</sup> I cried unto him with my mouth,<br>And he was extolled with my tongue.<br>
<sup>18</sup> If I regard iniquity in my heart,<br>The Lord will not hear _me:_<br>
<sup>19</sup> _But_ verily God hath heard _me_;<br>He hath attended to the voice of my prayer.<br>
<sup>20</sup> Blessed _be_ God, which hath not turned away my prayer,<br>Nor his mercy from me.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Heb. _all the earth_
> - <sup>b</sup> 3. Or, _yield feigned obedience, Heb. lie_
> - <sup>c</sup> 9. Heb. _putteth_
> - <sup>d</sup> 12. Heb. _moist_
> - <sup>e</sup> 14. Heb. _opened_
> - <sup>f</sup> 15. Heb. _marrow_

## Chapter 67

> _To the chief Musician on Neginoth, A Psalm _or_ Song._

<sup>1</sup> God be merciful unto us, and bless us;<br>_And_ cause his face to shine upon us;<sup>a</sup> _Selah._<br>
<sup>2</sup> That thy way may be known upon earth,<br>Thy saving health among all nations.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Let the people praise thee, O God;<br>Let all the people praise thee.<br>
<sup>4</sup> O let the nations be glad and sing for joy:<br>For thou shalt judge the people righteously, and govern<sup>b</sup> the nations upon earth. _Selah._<br>
<sup>5</sup> Let the people praise thee, O God;<br>Let all the people praise thee.<br>
<sup>6</sup> _Then_ shall the earth yield her increase;<br>_And_ God, _even_ our own God, shall bless us.<br>
<sup>7</sup> God shall bless us;<br>And all the ends of the earth shall fear him.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Heb. _with us_
> - <sup>b</sup> 4. Heb. _lead_

## Chapter 68

> _To the chief Musician, A Psalm _or_ Song of David._

<sup>1</sup> Let God arise, let his enemies be scattered:<br>Let them also that hate him flee before him.<sup>a</sup><br>
<sup>2</sup> As smoke is driven away, _so_ drive _them_ away:<br>As wax melteth before the fire, _so_ let the wicked perish at the presence of God.<br>
<sup>3</sup> But let the righteous be glad; let them rejoice before God:<br>Yea, let them exceedingly rejoice.<sup>b</sup><br>
<sup>4</sup> Sing unto God,<br>Sing praises to his name:<br>Extol him that rideth upon the heavens<br>By his name JAH, and rejoice before him.<br>
<sup>5</sup> A father of the fatherless, and a judge of the widows,<br>_Is_ God in his holy habitation.<br>
<sup>6</sup> God setteth the solitary in families:<sup>c</sup> He bringeth out those which are bound with chains:<br>But the rebellious dwell in a dry _land_.<br>
<sup>7</sup> O God, when thou wentest forth before thy people,<br>When thou didst march through the wilderness; _Selah:_<br>
<sup>8</sup> The earth shook, the heavens also dropped at the presence of God:<br>_Even_ Sinai itself _was moved_<br>At the presence of God, the God of Israel.<br>
<sup>9</sup> Thou, O God, didst send<sup>d</sup> a plentiful rain, whereby thou didst confirm<sup>e</sup> thine inheritance,<br>When it was weary.<br>
<sup>10</sup> Thy congregation hath dwelt therein:<br>Thou, O God, hast prepared of thy goodness for the poor.<br>
<sup>11</sup> The Lord gave the word:<br>Great _was_ the company<sup>f</sup> of those that published _it_.<br>
<sup>12</sup> Kings of armies did flee apace:<sup>g</sup><br>And she that tarried at home divided the spoil.<br>
<sup>13</sup> Though ye have lien among the pots, _yet shall ye be as_ the wings of a dove<br>Covered with silver, and her feathers with yellow gold.<br>
<sup>14</sup> When the Almighty scattered kings in it,<br>It was<sup>h</sup> _white_ as snow in Salmon.<br>
<sup>15</sup> The hill of God _is as_ the hill of Bashan;<br>An high hill _as_ the hill of Bashan.<br>
<sup>16</sup> Why leap ye, ye high hills? _This is_ the hill _which_ God desireth to dwell in;<br>Yea, the LORD will dwell _in it_ for ever.<br>
<sup>17</sup> The chariots of God _are_ twenty thousand, _even_ thousands<sup>i</sup> of angels:<br>The Lord _is_ among them, _as in_ Sinai, in the holy _place_.<br>
<sup>18</sup> Thou hast ascended on high, thou hast led captivity captive: Thou hast received gifts for men;<sup>j</sup><br>Yea, _for_ the rebellious also, that the Lord GOD might dwell _among them_.<br>
<sup>19</sup> Blessed _be_ the Lord,<br>_Who_ daily loadeth us _with benefits_, _even_ the God of our salvation. _Selah._<br>
<sup>20</sup> _He that is_ our God _is_ the God of salvation;<br>And unto GOD the Lord _belong_ the issues from death.<br>
<sup>21</sup> But God shall wound the head of his enemies,<br>_And_ the hairy scalp of such an one as goeth on still in his trespasses.<br>
<sup>22</sup> The Lord said, I will bring again from Bashan, I will bring _my people_ again<br>from the depths of the sea:<br>
<sup>23</sup> That thy foot may be dipped<sup>k</sup> in the blood of _thine_ enemies,<br>_And_ the tongue of thy dogs in the same.<br>
<sup>24</sup> They have seen thy goings, O God;<br>_Even_ the goings of my God, my King, in the sanctuary.<br>
<sup>25</sup> The singers went before, the players on instruments _followed_ after;<br>Among _them were_ the damsels playing with timbrels.<br>
<sup>26</sup> Bless ye God in the congregations,<br>_Even_ the Lord, from the fountain of Israel.<sup>l</sup><br>
<sup>27</sup> There _is_ little Benjamin _with_ their ruler, the princes of Judah _and_ their council,<sup>m</sup><br>The princes of Zebulun, _and_ the princes of Naphtali.<br>
<sup>28</sup> Thy God hath commanded thy strength:<br>Strengthen, O God, that which thou hast wrought for us.<br>
<sup>29</sup> Because of thy temple at Jerusalem<br>Shall kings bring presents unto thee.<br>
<sup>30</sup> Rebuke the company of spearmen,<sup>n</sup><br>The multitude of the bulls, with the calves of the people,<br>_Till every one_ submit himself with pieces of silver:<br>Scatter thou<sup>o</sup> the people _that_ delight in war.<br>
<sup>31</sup> Princes shall come out of Egypt;<br>Ethiopia shall soon stretch out her hands unto God.<br>
<sup>32</sup> Sing unto God, ye kingdoms of the earth;<br>O sing praises unto the Lord; _Selah:_<br>
<sup>33</sup> To him that rideth upon the heavens of heavens, _which were_ of old;<br>Lo, he doth send out<sup>p</sup> his voice, _and that_ a mighty voice.<br>
<sup>34</sup> Ascribe ye strength unto God: His excellency _is_ over Israel,<br>And his strength _is_ in the clouds.<sup>q</sup><br>
<sup>35</sup> O God, _thou art_ terrible out of thy holy places:<br>The God of Israel _is_ he that giveth strength and power unto _his_ people. Blessed _be_ God.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Heb. _from his face_
> - <sup>b</sup> 3. Heb. _rejoice with gladness_
> - <sup>c</sup> 6. Heb. _in a house_
> - <sup>d</sup> 9. Heb. _shake out_
> - <sup>e</sup> 9. Heb. _confirm it_
> - <sup>f</sup> 11. Heb. _army_
> - <sup>g</sup> 12. Heb. _did flee, did flee_
> - <sup>h</sup> 14. Or, _for her, she was_
> - <sup>i</sup> 17. Or, _even many thousands_
> - <sup>j</sup> 18. Heb. _in the man_
> - <sup>k</sup> 23. Or, _red_
> - <sup>l</sup> 26. Or, _ye that are of the fountain of Israel_
> - <sup>m</sup> 27. Or, _with their company_
> - <sup>n</sup> 30. Or, _the beast of the reeds_
> - <sup>o</sup> 30. Or, _he scattereth_
> - <sup>p</sup> 33. Heb. _give_
> - <sup>q</sup> 34. Or, _heavens_

## Chapter 69

> _To the chief Musician upon Shoshannim, _A Psalm_ of David._

<sup>1</sup> Save me, O God;<br>For the waters are come in unto _my_ soul.<br>
<sup>2</sup> I sink in deep mire,<sup>a</sup> where _there is_ no standing:<br>I am come into deep waters,<sup>b</sup> where the floods overflow me.<br>
<sup>3</sup> I am weary of my crying: my throat is dried:<br>Mine eyes fail while I wait for my God.<br>
<sup>4</sup> They that hate me without a cause are more than the hairs of mine head:<br>They that would destroy me, _being_ mine enemies wrongfully, are mighty:<br>Then I restored _that_ which I took not away.<br>
<sup>5</sup> O God, thou knowest my foolishness; And my sins<sup>c</sup> are not hid from thee.<br>
<sup>6</sup> Let not them that wait on thee, O Lord GOD of hosts, be ashamed for my sake:<br>Let not those that seek thee be confounded for my sake, O God of Israel.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Because for thy sake I have borne reproach;<br>Shame hath covered my face.<br>
<sup>8</sup> I am become a stranger unto my brethren,<br>And an alien unto my mother’s children.<br>
<sup>9</sup> For the zeal of thine house hath eaten me up;<br>And the reproaches of them that reproached thee are fallen upon me.<br>
<sup>10</sup> When I wept, _and chastened_ my soul with fasting,<br>That was to my reproach.<br>
<sup>11</sup> I made sackcloth also my garment;<br>And I became a proverb to them.<br>
<sup>12</sup> They that sit in the gate speak against me;<br>And I _was_ the song of the drunkards.<sup>d</sup><br>
<sup>13</sup> But as for me, my prayer<br>_Is_ unto thee, O LORD, _in_ an acceptable time:<br>O God, in the multitude of thy mercy<br>Hear me, in the truth of thy salvation.<br>
<sup>14</sup> Deliver me out of the mire, and let me not sink:<br>Let me be delivered from them that hate me, and out of the deep waters.<br>
<sup>15</sup> Let not the waterflood overflow me, neither let the deep swallow me up,<br>And let not the pit shut her mouth upon me.<br>
<sup>16</sup> Hear me, O LORD; for thy lovingkindness _is_ good:<br>Turn unto me according to the multitude of thy tender mercies.<br>
<sup>17</sup> And hide not thy face from thy servant; For I am in trouble:<br>Hear me speedily.<sup>e</sup><br>
<sup>18</sup> Draw nigh unto my soul, _and_ redeem it:<br>Deliver me because of mine enemies.<br>
<sup>19</sup> Thou hast known my reproach, and my shame, and my dishonour:<br>Mine adversaries _are_ all before thee.<br>
<sup>20</sup> Reproach hath broken my heart; and I am full of heaviness:<br>And I looked _for some_ to take pity,<sup>f</sup> but _there was_ none; And for comforters, but I found none.<br>
<sup>21</sup> They gave me also gall for my meat;<br>And in my thirst they gave me vinegar to drink.<br>
<sup>22</sup> Let their table become a snare before them:<br>And _that which should have been_ for _their_ welfare,<br>_let it become_ a trap.<br>
<sup>23</sup> Let their eyes be darkened, that they see not;<br>And make their loins continually to shake.<br>
<sup>24</sup> Pour out thine indignation upon them,<br>And let thy wrathful anger take hold of them.<br>
<sup>25</sup> Let their habitation<sup>g</sup> be desolate;<br>_And_ let none dwell<sup>h</sup> in their tents.<br>
<sup>26</sup> For they persecute _him_ whom thou hast smitten;<br>And they talk to the grief of those whom thou hast wounded.<sup>i</sup><br>
<sup>27</sup> Add iniquity unto their iniquity:<sup>j</sup><br>And let them not come into thy righteousness.<br>
<sup>28</sup> Let them be blotted out of the book of the living,<br>And not be written with the righteous.<br>
<sup>29</sup> But I _am_ poor and sorrowful:<br>Let thy salvation, O God, set me up on high.<br>
<sup>30</sup> I will praise the name of God with a song,<br>And will magnify him with thanksgiving.<br>
<sup>31</sup> _This_ also shall please the LORD<br>Better than an ox _or_ bullock that hath horns and hoofs.<br>
<sup>32</sup> The humble<sup>k</sup> shall see _this, and_ be glad:<br>And your heart shall live that seek God.<br>
<sup>33</sup> For the LORD heareth the poor,<br>And despiseth not his prisoners.<br>
<sup>34</sup> Let the heaven and earth praise him,<br>The seas, and every thing that moveth<sup>l</sup> therein.<br>
<sup>35</sup> For God will save Zion, and will build the cities of Judah:<br>That they may dwell there, and have it in possession.<br>
<sup>36</sup> The seed also of his servants shall inherit it:<br>And they that love his name shall dwell therein.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 2. Heb. _the mire of depth_
> - <sup>b</sup> 2. Heb. _depth of waters_
> - <sup>c</sup> 5. Heb. _guiltiness_
> - <sup>d</sup> 12. Heb. _drinkers of strong drink_
> - <sup>e</sup> 17. Heb. _make haste to hear me_
> - <sup>f</sup> 20. Heb. _to lament with me_
> - <sup>g</sup> 25. Heb. _their palace_
> - <sup>h</sup> 25. Heb. _let there not be a dweller_
> - <sup>i</sup> 26. Heb. _thy wounded_
> - <sup>j</sup> 27. Or, _punishment of iniquity_
> - <sup>k</sup> 32. Or, _meek_
> - <sup>l</sup> 34. Heb. _creepeth_

## Chapter 70

> _To the chief Musician, _A Psalm_ of David, to bring to remembrance._

<sup>1</sup> _Make haste_, O God, to deliver me;<br>Make haste to help me,<sup>a</sup> O LORD.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Let them be ashamed and confounded that seek after my soul:<br>Let them be turned backward, and put to confusion, that desire my hurt.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Let them be turned back for a reward of their shame<br>That say, Aha, aha.<br>
<sup>4</sup> Let all those that seek thee rejoice and be glad in thee:<br>And let such as love thy salvation say continually, let God be magnified.<br>
<sup>5</sup> But I _am_ poor and needy;<br>Make haste unto me, O God:<br>Thou _art_ my help and my deliverer;<br>O LORD, make no tarrying.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Heb. _to my help_

## Chapter 71

<sup>1</sup> In thee, O LORD, do I put my trust: Let me never be put to confusion.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Deliver me in thy righteousness, and cause me to escape: Incline thine ear unto me, and save me.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Be thou my strong habitation,<sup>a</sup> whereunto I may continually resort:<br>Thou hast given commandment to save me; For thou _art_ my rock and my fortress.<br>
<sup>4</sup> Deliver me, O my God, out of the hand of the wicked,<br>Out of the hand of the unrighteous and cruel man.<br>
<sup>5</sup> For thou _art_ my hope, O Lord GOD:<br>_Thou art_ my trust from my youth.<br>
<sup>6</sup> By thee have I been holden up from the womb:<br>Thou art he that took me out of my mother’s bowels: My praise _shall be_ continually of thee.<br>
<sup>7</sup> I am as a wonder unto many;<br>But thou _art_ my strong refuge.<br>
<sup>8</sup> Let my mouth be filled _with_ thy praise<br>_And with_ thy honour all the day.<br>
<sup>9</sup> Cast me not off in the time of old age;<br>Forsake me not when my strength faileth.<br>
<sup>10</sup> For mine enemies speak against me;<br>And they that lay wait<sup>b</sup> for my soul take counsel together,
<sup>11</sup> saying,<br>God hath forsaken him: Persecute and take him; for _there is_ none to deliver _him_.<br>
<sup>12</sup> O God, be not far from me:<br>O my God, make haste for my help.<br>
<sup>13</sup> Let them be confounded _and_ consumed that are adversaries to my soul;<br>Let them be covered _with_ reproach and dishonour that seek my hurt.<br>
<sup>14</sup> But I will hope continually,<br>And will yet praise thee more and more.<br>
<sup>15</sup> My mouth shall shew forth thy righteousness _and_ thy salvation all the day;<br>For I know not the numbers _thereof_.<br>
<sup>16</sup> I will go in the strength of the Lord GOD:<br>I will make mention of thy righteousness, _even_ of thine only.<br>
<sup>17</sup> O God, thou hast taught me from my youth:<br>And hitherto have I declared thy wondrous works.<br>
<sup>18</sup> Now also when I am old and greyheaded, O God, forsake me not;<br>Until I have shewed thy strength<sup>c</sup> unto _this_ generation, _and_ thy power to every one _that_ is to come.<br>
<sup>19</sup> Thy righteousness also, O God, _is_ very high,<br>Who hast done great things: O God, who _is_ like unto thee!<br>
<sup>20</sup> _Thou_, which hast shewed me great and sore troubles, shalt quicken me again,<br>And shalt bring me up again from the depths of the earth.<br>
<sup>21</sup> Thou shalt increase my greatness,<br>And comfort me on every side.<br>
<sup>22</sup> I will also praise thee with the psaltery,<sup>d</sup> _even_ thy truth, O my God:<br>Unto thee will I sing with the harp, O thou Holy One of Israel.<br>
<sup>23</sup> My lips shall greatly rejoice when I sing unto thee;<br>And my soul, which thou hast redeemed.<br>
<sup>24</sup> My tongue also shall talk of thy righteousness all the day long:<br>For they are confounded, for they are brought unto shame, that seek my hurt.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 3. Heb. _be thou to me for a rock of habitation_
> - <sup>b</sup> 10. Heb. _watch_, or _observe_
> - <sup>c</sup> 18. Heb. _thine arm_
> - <sup>d</sup> 22. Heb. _with the instrument of psaltery_

## Chapter 72

> __A Psalm_ for Solomon._

<sup>1</sup> Give the king thy judgments, O God,<br>And thy righteousness unto the king’s son.<br>
<sup>2</sup> He shall judge thy people with righteousness,<br>And thy poor with judgment.<br>
<sup>3</sup> The mountains shall bring peace to the people,<br>And the little hills, by righteousness.<br>
<sup>4</sup> He shall judge the poor of the people,<br>He shall save the children of the needy,<br>And shall break in pieces the oppressor.<br>
<sup>5</sup> They shall fear thee as long as the sun and moon endure,<br>Throughout all generations.<br>
<sup>6</sup> He shall come down like rain upon the mown grass:<br>As showers _that_ water the earth.<br>
<sup>7</sup> In his days shall the righteous flourish;<br>And abundance of peace so long as<sup>a</sup> the moon endureth.<br>
<sup>8</sup> He shall have dominion also from sea to sea,<br>And from the river unto the ends of the earth.<br>
<sup>9</sup> They that dwell in the wilderness shall bow before him;<br>And his enemies shall lick the dust.<br>
<sup>10</sup> The kings of Tarshish and of the isles shall bring presents:<br>The kings of Sheba and Seba shall offer gifts.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Yea, all kings shall fall down before him:<br>All nations shall serve him.<br>
<sup>12</sup> For he shall deliver the needy when he crieth;<br>The poor also, and _him_ that hath no helper.<br>
<sup>13</sup> He shall spare the poor and needy,<br>And shall save the souls of the needy.<br>
<sup>14</sup> He shall redeem their soul from deceit and violence:<br>And precious shall their blood be in his sight.<br>
<sup>15</sup> And he shall live, and to him shall be given<sup>b</sup> of the gold of Sheba:<br>Prayer also shall be made for him continually; _and_ daily shall he be praised.<br>
<sup>16</sup> There shall be an handful of corn in the earth upon the top of the mountains;<br>The fruit thereof shall shake like Lebanon: And _they_ of the city shall flourish like grass of the earth.<br>
<sup>17</sup> His name shall endure<sup>c</sup> for ever:<br>His name shall be continued as long as the sun:<br>And _men_ shall be blessed in him: All nations shall call him blessed.<br>
<sup>18</sup> Blessed _be_ the LORD God, the God of Israel,<br>Who only doeth wondrous things.<br>
<sup>19</sup> And blessed _be_ his glorious name for ever:<br>And let the whole earth be filled _with_ his glory; Amen, and Amen.<br>
<sup>20</sup> The prayers of David the son of Jesse are ended.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 7. Heb. _till there be no moon_
> - <sup>b</sup> 15. Heb. _one shall give_
> - <sup>c</sup> 17. Heb. _shall be_

## Chapter 73

> _A Psalm of Asaph._

<sup>1</sup> Truly<sup>a</sup> God _is_ good to Israel,<br>_Even_ to such as are of a clean heart.<sup>b</sup><br>
<sup>2</sup> But as for me, my feet were almost gone;<br>My steps had well nigh slipped.<br>
<sup>3</sup> For I was envious at the foolish,<br>_When_ I saw the prosperity of the wicked.<br>
<sup>4</sup> For _there are_ no bands in their death:<br>But their strength _is_ firm.<sup>c</sup><br>
<sup>5</sup> They _are_ not in trouble _as other_ men;<sup>d</sup><br>Neither are they plagued like<sup>e</sup> _other_ men.<br>
<sup>6</sup> Therefore pride compasseth them about as a chain;<br>Violence covereth them _as_ a garment.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Their eyes stand out with fatness:<br>They have more than heart could wish.<br>
<sup>8</sup> They are corrupt, and speak wickedly _concerning_ oppression:<br>They speak loftily.<br>
<sup>9</sup> They set their mouth against the heavens,<br>And their tongue walketh through the earth.<br>
<sup>10</sup> Therefore his people return hither:<br>And waters of a full _cup_ are wrung out to them.<br>
<sup>11</sup> And they say, How doth God know?<br>And is there knowledge in the most High?<br>
<sup>12</sup> Behold, these _are_ the ungodly, who prosper in the world; they increase _in_ riches.<br>
<sup>13</sup> Verily I have cleansed my heart _in_ vain, and washed my hands in innocency.<br>
<sup>14</sup> For all the day long have I been plagued,<br>And chastened<sup>f</sup> every morning.<br>
<sup>15</sup> If I say, I will speak thus;<br>Behold, I should offend _against_ the generation of thy children.<br>
<sup>16</sup> When I thought to know this,<br>It _was_ too painful for me;<sup>g</sup><br>
<sup>17</sup> Until I went into the sanctuary of God;<br>_Then_ understood I their end.<br>
<sup>18</sup> Surely thou didst set them in slippery places:<br>Thou castedst them down into destruction.<br>
<sup>19</sup> How are they _brought_ into desolation, as in a moment!<br>They are utterly consumed with terrors.<br>
<sup>20</sup> As a dream when _one_ awaketh;<br>_So_, O Lord, when thou awakest, thou shalt despise their image.<br>
<sup>21</sup> Thus my heart was grieved,<br>And I was pricked in my reins.<br>
<sup>22</sup> So foolish _was_ I, and ignorant:<sup>h</sup><br>I was _as_ a beast before thee.<sup>i</sup><br>
<sup>23</sup> Nevertheless I _am_ continually with thee:<br>Thou hast holden _me_ by my right hand.<br>
<sup>24</sup> Thou shalt guide me with thy counsel,<br>And afterward receive me _to_ glory.<br>
<sup>25</sup> Whom have I in heaven _but thee?_<br>And _there is_ none upon earth _that_ I desire beside thee.<br>
<sup>26</sup> My flesh and my heart faileth:<br>_But_ God _is_ the strength<sup>j</sup> of my heart, and my portion for ever.<br>
<sup>27</sup> For, lo, they that are far from thee shall perish:<br>Thou hast destroyed all them that go a whoring from thee.<br>
<sup>28</sup> But _it is_ good for me to draw near to God: I have put my trust in the Lord GOD,<br>That I may declare all thy works.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Or, _yet_
> - <sup>b</sup> 1. Heb. _clean of heart_
> - <sup>c</sup> 4. Heb. _fat_
> - <sup>d</sup> 5. Heb. _in the trouble of other men_
> - <sup>e</sup> 5. Heb. _with_
> - <sup>f</sup> 14. Heb. _my chastisement was_
> - <sup>g</sup> 16. Heb. _it was labour in mine eyes_
> - <sup>h</sup> 22. Heb. _I knew not_
> - <sup>i</sup> 22. Heb. _with thee_
> - <sup>j</sup> 26. Heb. _rock_

## Chapter 74

> _Maschil of Asaph._

<sup>1</sup> O God, why hast thou cast _us_ off for ever?<br>_Why_ doth thine anger smoke against the sheep of thy pasture?<br>
