<sup>2</sup> Remember thy congregation, _which_ thou hast purchased of old;<br>The rod<sup>a</sup> of thine inheritance, _which_ thou hast redeemed;<br>This mount Zion, wherein thou hast dwelt.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Lift up thy feet unto the perpetual desolations;<br>_Even_ all _that_ the enemy<br>hath done wickedly in the sanctuary.<br>
<sup>4</sup> Thine enemies roar in the midst of thy congregations;<br>They set up their ensigns _for_ signs.<br>
<sup>5</sup> _A man_ was famous according as he had lifted up axes upon the thick trees.<br>
<sup>6</sup> But now they break down the carved work<br>Thereof at once with axes and hammers.<br>
<sup>7</sup> They have cast fire into thy sanctuary,<sup>b</sup><br>They have defiled _by casting down_ the dwelling place of thy name to the ground.<br>
<sup>8</sup> They said in their hearts, Let us destroy<sup>c</sup> them together:<br>They have burned up all the synagogues of God in the land.<br>
<sup>9</sup> We see not our signs: _There is_ no more any prophet:<br>Neither _is there_ among us any that knoweth how long.<br>
<sup>10</sup> O God, how long shall the adversary reproach?<br>Shall the enemy blaspheme thy name for ever?<br>
<sup>11</sup> Why withdrawest thou thy hand, even thy right hand?<br>Pluck _it_ out of thy bosom.<br>
<sup>12</sup> For God _is_ my King of old,<br>Working salvation in the midst of the earth.<br>
<sup>13</sup> Thou didst divide<sup>d</sup> the sea by thy strength:<br>Thou brakest the heads of the dragons<sup>e</sup> in the waters.<br>
<sup>14</sup> Thou brakest the heads of leviathan in pieces,<br>_And_ gavest him _to be_ meat to the people inhabiting the wilderness.<br>
<sup>15</sup> Thou didst cleave the fountain and the flood:<br>Thou driedst up mighty rivers.<sup>f</sup><br>
<sup>16</sup> The day _is_ thine, the night also _is_ thine:<br>Thou hast prepared the light and the sun.<br>
<sup>17</sup> Thou hast set all the borders of the earth:<br>Thou hast made<sup>g</sup> summer and winter.<br>
<sup>18</sup> Remember this, _that_ the enemy hath reproached, O LORD,<br>And _that_ the foolish people have blasphemed thy name.<br>
<sup>19</sup> O deliver not the soul of thy turtledove unto the multitude _of the wicked:_<br>Forget not the congregation of thy poor for ever.<br>
<sup>20</sup> Have respect unto the covenant:<br>For the dark places of the earth are full of the habitations of cruelty.<br>
<sup>21</sup> O let not the oppressed return ashamed:<br>Let the poor and needy praise thy name.<br>
<sup>22</sup> Arise, O God, plead thine own cause:<br>Remember how the foolish man reproacheth thee daily.<br>
<sup>23</sup> Forget not the voice of thine enemies:<br>The tumult of those that rise up against thee increaseth<sup>h</sup> continually.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 2. Or, _tribe_
> - <sup>b</sup> 7. Heb. _they have sent thy sanctuary into the fire_
> - <sup>c</sup> 8. Heb. _break_
> - <sup>d</sup> 13. Heb. _break_
> - <sup>e</sup> 13. Or, _whales_
> - <sup>f</sup> 15. Heb. _rivers of strength_
> - <sup>g</sup> 17. Heb. _made them_
> - <sup>h</sup> 23. Heb. _ascendeth_

## Chapter 75

> _To the chief Musician, Al-taschith, A Psalm _or_ Song of Asaph._

<sup>1</sup> Unto thee, O God, do we give thanks,<br>_Unto thee_ do we give thanks:<br>For _that_ thy name is near<br>Thy wondrous works declare.<br>
<sup>2</sup> When I shall receive<sup>a</sup> the congregation<br>I will judge uprightly.<br>
<sup>3</sup> The earth and all the inhabitants thereof are dissolved:<br>I bear up the pillars of it. _Selah._<br>
<sup>4</sup> I said unto the fools, Deal not foolishly:<br>And to the wicked, Lift not up the horn:<br>
<sup>5</sup> Lift not up your horn on high:<br>Speak _not_ with a stiff neck.<br>
<sup>6</sup> For promotion _cometh_ neither from the east, nor from the west,<br>Nor from the south.<sup>b</sup><br>
<sup>7</sup> But God _is_ the judge:<br>He putteth down one, and setteth up another.<br>
<sup>8</sup> For in the hand of the LORD _there is_ a cup, and the wine is red;<br>It is full of mixture; and he poureth out of the same:<br>But the dregs thereof,<br>All the wicked of the earth shall wring _them_ out, _and_ drink _them_.<br>
<sup>9</sup> But I will declare for ever; I will sing praises to the God of Jacob.<br>
<sup>10</sup> All the horns of the wicked also will I cut off;<br>_But_ the horns of the righteous shall be exalted.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 2. Or, _when I shall take a set time_
> - <sup>b</sup> 6. Heb. _desert_

## Chapter 76

> _To the chief Musician on Neginoth, A Psalm _or_ Song of Asaph._

<sup>1</sup> In Judah _is_ God known:<br>His name _is_ great in Israel.<br>
<sup>2</sup> In Salem also is his tabernacle,<br>And his dwelling place in Zion.<br>
<sup>3</sup> There brake he the arrows of the bow,<br>The shield, and the sword, and the battle. _Selah._<br>
<sup>4</sup> Thou _art_ more glorious _and_ excellent<br>Than the mountains of prey.<br>
<sup>5</sup> The stouthearted are spoiled, they have slept their sleep:<br>And none of the men of might have found their hands.<br>
<sup>6</sup> At thy rebuke, O God of Jacob,<br>Both the chariot and horse are cast into a dead sleep.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Thou, _even_ thou, _art_ to be feared:<br>And who may stand in thy sight when once thou art angry?<br>
<sup>8</sup> Thou didst cause judgment to be heard from heaven;<br>The earth feared, and was still,<br>
<sup>9</sup> When God arose to judgment,<br>To save all the meek of the earth. _Selah._<br>
<sup>10</sup> Surely the wrath of man shall praise thee:<br>The remainder of wrath shalt thou restrain.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Vow, and pay unto the LORD your God: Let all that be round about him<br>Bring presents unto him that ought to be feared.<sup>a</sup><br>
<sup>12</sup> He shall cut off the spirit of princes:<br>_He is_ terrible to the kings of the earth.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 11. Heb. _to Fear_

## Chapter 77

> _To the chief Musician, to Jeduthun, A Psalm of Asaph._

<sup>1</sup> I Cried unto God with my voice,<br>_Even_ unto God with my voice; and he gave ear unto me.<br>
<sup>2</sup> In the day of my trouble I sought the Lord:<br>My sore<sup>a</sup> ran in the night, and ceased not: My soul refused to be comforted.<br>
<sup>3</sup> I remembered God, and was troubled:<br>I complained, and my spirit was overwhelmed. _Selah._<br>
<sup>4</sup> Thou holdest mine eyes waking:<br>I am so troubled that I cannot speak.<br>
<sup>5</sup> I have considered the days of old,<br>The years of ancient times.<br>
<sup>6</sup> I call to remembrance my song in the night:<br>I commune with mine own heart: And my spirit made diligent search.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Will the Lord cast off for ever?<br>And will he be favourable no more?<br>
<sup>8</sup> Is his mercy clean gone for ever?<br>Doth _his_ promise fail for evermore?<br>
<sup>9</sup> Hath God forgotten to be gracious?<br>Hath he in anger shut up his tender mercies? _Selah._<br>
<sup>10</sup> And I said, This _is_ my infirmity:<br>_But I will remember_ the years of the right hand of the Most High.<br>
<sup>11</sup> I will remember the works of the LORD:<br>Surely I will remember thy wonders of old.<br>
<sup>12</sup> I will meditate also of all thy work,<br>And talk of thy doings.<br>
<sup>13</sup> Thy way, O God, _is_ in the sanctuary:<br>Who _is so_ great a God as _our_ God?<br>
<sup>14</sup> Thou _art_ the God that doest wonders:<br>Thou hast declared thy strength among the people.<br>
<sup>15</sup> Thou hast with _thine_ arm redeemed thy people,<br>The sons of Jacob and Joseph. _Selah._<br>
<sup>16</sup> The waters saw thee, O God, the waters saw thee; they were afraid:<br>The depths also were troubled.<br>
<sup>17</sup> The clouds poured out water:<sup>b</sup> The skies sent out a sound:<br>Thine arrows also went abroad.<br>
<sup>18</sup> The voice of thy thunder _was_ in the heaven:<br>The lightnings lightened the world: The earth trembled and shook.<br>
<sup>19</sup> Thy way _is_ in the sea, and thy path in the great waters,<br>And thy footsteps are not known.<br>
<sup>20</sup> Thou leddest thy people like a flock<br>By the hand of Moses and Aaron.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 2. Heb. _my hand_
> - <sup>b</sup> 17. Heb. _the clouds were poured forth with water_

## Chapter 78

> _Maschil of Asaph._

<sup>1</sup> Give ear, O my people, _to_ my law:<br>Incline your ears to the words of my mouth.<br>
<sup>2</sup> I will open my mouth in a parable:<br>I will utter dark sayings of old:<br>
<sup>3</sup> Which we have heard and known,<br>And our fathers have told us.<br>
<sup>4</sup> We will not hide _them_ from their children, shewing to the generation to come,<br>The praises of the LORD, and his strength, and his wonderful works that he hath done.<br>
<sup>5</sup> For he established a testimony in Jacob, and appointed a law in Israel,<br>Which he commanded our fathers, that they should make them known to their children:<br>
<sup>6</sup> That the generation to come might know _them_,<br>_Even_ the children _which_ should be born;<br>_Who_ should arise<br>And declare _them_ to their children:<br>
<sup>7</sup> That they might set their hope in God,<br>And not forget the works of God, but keep his commandments:<br>
<sup>8</sup> And might not be as their fathers, a stubborn and rebellious generation;<br>A generation _that_ set not their heart aright,<sup>a</sup> and whose spirit was not stedfast with God.<br>
<sup>9</sup> The children of Ephraim, _being_ armed, _and_ carrying<sup>b</sup> bows,<br>Turned back in the day of battle.<br>
<sup>10</sup> They kept not the covenant of God,<br>And refused to walk in his law;<br>
<sup>11</sup> And forgat his works,<br>And his wonders that he had shewed them.<br>
<sup>12</sup> Marvellous things did he in the sight of their fathers, in the land of Egypt,<br>_In_ the field of Zoan.<br>
<sup>13</sup> He divided the sea, and caused them to pass through;<br>And he made the waters to stand as an heap.<br>
<sup>14</sup> In the daytime also he led them with a cloud,<br>And all the night with a light of fire.<br>
<sup>15</sup> He clave the rocks in the wilderness,<br>And gave _them_ drink as _out of_ the great depths.<br>
<sup>16</sup> He brought streams also out of the rock,<br>And caused waters to run down like rivers.<br>
<sup>17</sup> And they sinned yet more against him<br>By provoking the most High in the wilderness.<br>
<sup>18</sup> And they tempted God in their heart<br>By asking meat for their lust.<br>
<sup>19</sup> Yea, they spake against God;<br>They said, Can God furnish<sup>c</sup> a table in the wilderness?<br>
<sup>20</sup> Behold, he smote the rock, that the waters gushed out, and the streams overflowed;<br>Can he give bread also? Can he provide flesh for his people?<br>
<sup>21</sup> Therefore the LORD heard _this_, and was wroth:<br>So a fire was kindled against Jacob, and anger also came up against Israel;<br>
<sup>22</sup> Because they believed not in God,<br>And trusted not in his salvation:<br>
<sup>23</sup> Though he had commanded the clouds from above,<br>And opened the doors of heaven,<br>
<sup>24</sup> And had rained down manna upon them to eat,<br>And had given them of the corn of heaven.<br>
<sup>25</sup> Man did eat angels’ food:<br>He sent them meat to the full.<br>
<sup>26</sup> He caused an east wind to blow<sup>d</sup> in the heaven:<br>And by his power he brought in the south wind.<br>
<sup>27</sup> He rained flesh also upon them as dust,<br>And feathered fowls like as the sand of the sea:<br>
<sup>28</sup> And he let _it_ fall in the midst of their camp,<br>Round about their habitations.<br>
<sup>29</sup> So they did eat, and were well filled: For he gave them their own desire;<br>
<sup>30</sup> They were not estranged from their lust.<br>But while their meat _was_ yet in their mouths,<br>
<sup>31</sup> The wrath of God came upon them, and slew the fattest of them,<br>And smote down<sup>e</sup> the chosen _men_<sup>f</sup> of Israel.<br>
<sup>32</sup> For all this they sinned still,<br>And believed not for his wondrous works.<br>
<sup>33</sup> Therefore their days did he consume in vanity,<br>And their years in trouble.<br>
<sup>34</sup> When he slew them, then they sought him:<br>And they returned and enquired early after God.<br>
<sup>35</sup> And they remembered that God _was_ their rock,<br>And the high God their redeemer.<br>
<sup>36</sup> Nevertheless they did flatter him with their mouth,<br>And they lied unto him with their tongues.<br>
<sup>37</sup> For their heart was not right with him,<br>Neither were they stedfast in his covenant.<br>
<sup>38</sup> But he, _being_ full of compassion,<br>Forgave _their_ iniquity, and destroyed _them_ not:<br>Yea, many a time turned he his anger away,<br>And did not stir up all his wrath.<br>
<sup>39</sup> For he remembered that they _were but_ flesh;<br>A wind that passeth away, and cometh not again.<br>
<sup>40</sup> How oft did they provoke him<sup>g</sup> in the wilderness,<br>_And_ grieve him in the desert!<br>
<sup>41</sup> Yea, they turned back and tempted God,<br>And limited the Holy One of Israel.<br>
<sup>42</sup> They remembered not his hand,<br>_Nor_ the day when he delivered them from the enemy.<sup>h</sup><br>
<sup>43</sup> How he had wrought<sup>i</sup> his signs in Egypt,<br>And his wonders in the field of Zoan:<br>
<sup>44</sup> And had turned their rivers into blood;<br>And their floods, that they could not drink.<br>
<sup>45</sup> He sent divers sorts of flies among them, which devoured them;<br>And frogs, which destroyed them.<br>
<sup>46</sup> He gave also their increase unto the caterpiller,<br>And their labour unto the locust.<br>
<sup>47</sup> He destroyed<sup>j</sup> their vines with hail,<br>And their sycomore trees with frost.<sup>k</sup><br>
<sup>48</sup> He gave up<sup>l</sup> their cattle also to the hail,<br>And their flocks to hot thunderbolts.<sup>m</sup><br>
<sup>49</sup> He cast upon them the fierceness of his anger, wrath, and indignation, and trouble,<br>By sending evil angels _among them_.<br>
<sup>50</sup> He made a way<sup>n</sup> to his anger; He spared not their soul from death,<br>But gave their life over to the pestilence;<sup>o</sup><br>
<sup>51</sup> And smote all the firstborn in Egypt;<br>The chief of _their_ strength in the tabernacles of Ham:<br>
<sup>52</sup> But made his own people to go forth like sheep,<br>And guided them in the wilderness like a flock.<br>
<sup>53</sup> And he led them on safely, so that they feared not:<br>But the sea overwhelmed<sup>p</sup> their enemies.<br>
<sup>54</sup> And he brought them to the border of his sanctuary,<br>_Even to_ this mountain, _which_ his right hand had purchased.<br>
<sup>55</sup> He cast out the heathen also before them,<br>And divided them an inheritance by line, and made the tribes of Israel to dwell in their tents.<br>
<sup>56</sup> Yet they tempted and provoked the most high God,<br>And kept not his testimonies:<br>
<sup>57</sup> But turned back, and dealt unfaithfully like their fathers:<br>They were turned aside like a deceitful bow.<br>
<sup>58</sup> For they provoked him to anger with their high places,<br>And moved him to jealousy with their graven images.<br>
<sup>59</sup> When God heard _this_, he was wroth,<br>And greatly abhorred Israel:<br>
<sup>60</sup> So that he forsook the tabernacle of Shiloh,<br>The tent _which_ he placed among men;<br>
<sup>61</sup> And delivered his strength into captivity,<br>And his glory into the enemy’s hand.<br>
<sup>62</sup> He gave his people over also unto the sword;<br>And was wroth with his inheritance.<br>
<sup>63</sup> The fire consumed their young men;<br>And their maidens were not given<sup>q</sup> to marriage.<br>
<sup>64</sup> Their priests fell by the sword;<br>And their widows made no lamentation.<br>
<sup>65</sup> Then the Lord awaked as one out of sleep,<br>_And_ like a mighty man that shouteth by reason of wine.<br>
<sup>66</sup> And he smote his enemies in the hinder parts:<br>He put them to a perpetual reproach.<br>
<sup>67</sup> Moreover he refused the tabernacle of Joseph,<br>And chose not the tribe of Ephraim:<br>
<sup>68</sup> But chose the tribe of Judah,<br>The mount Zion which he loved.<br>
<sup>69</sup> And he built his sanctuary like high _palaces_,<br>Like the earth which he hath established<sup>r</sup> for ever.<br>
<sup>70</sup> He chose David also his servant,<br>And took him from the sheepfolds:<br>
<sup>71</sup> From following<sup>s</sup> the ewes great with young he brought him<br>To feed Jacob his people, and Israel his inheritance.<br>
<sup>72</sup> So he fed them according to the integrity of his heart;<br>And guided them by the skilfulness of his hands.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 8. Heb. _that prepared not their heart_
> - <sup>b</sup> 9. Heb. _throwing forth_
> - <sup>c</sup> 19. Heb. _order_
> - <sup>d</sup> 26. Heb. _to go_
> - <sup>e</sup> 31. Heb. _made to bow_
> - <sup>f</sup> 31. Or, _young men_
> - <sup>g</sup> 40. Or, _rebel against him_
> - <sup>h</sup> 42. Or, _from affliction_
> - <sup>i</sup> 43. Heb. _set_
> - <sup>j</sup> 47. Heb. _killed_
> - <sup>k</sup> 47. Or, _great hail stones_
> - <sup>l</sup> 48. Heb. _he shut up_
> - <sup>m</sup> 48. Or, _lightnings_
> - <sup>n</sup> 50. Heb. _He weighed a path_
> - <sup>o</sup> 50. Or, _their beasts, to the murreine_
> - <sup>p</sup> 53. Heb. _covered_
> - <sup>q</sup> 63. Heb. _praised_
> - <sup>r</sup> 69. Heb. _founded_
> - <sup>s</sup> 71. Heb. _from after_

## Chapter 79

> _A Psalm of Asaph._

<sup>1</sup> O God, the heathen are come into thine inheritance;<br>Thy holy temple have they defiled; They have laid Jerusalem on heaps.<br>
<sup>2</sup> The dead bodies of thy servants have they given _to be_ meat unto the fowls of the heaven,<br>The flesh of thy saints unto the beasts of the earth.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Their blood have they shed like water round about Jerusalem;<br>And _there was_ none to bury _them_.<br>
<sup>4</sup> We are become a reproach to our neighbours,<br>A scorn and derision to them that are round about us.<br>
<sup>5</sup> How long, LORD? Wilt thou be angry for ever?<br>Shall thy jealousy burn like fire?<br>
<sup>6</sup> Pour out thy wrath upon the heathen that have not known thee,<br>And upon the kingdoms that have not called upon thy name.<br>
<sup>7</sup> For they have devoured Jacob,<br>And laid waste his dwelling place.<br>
<sup>8</sup> O remember not against us former iniquities:<sup>a</sup> Let thy tender mercies speedily prevent us:<br>For we are brought very low.<br>
<sup>9</sup> Help us, O God of our salvation, for the glory of thy name:<br>And deliver us, and purge away our sins, for thy name’s sake.<br>
<sup>10</sup> Wherefore should the heathen say,<br>Where _is_ their God?<br>Let him be known among the heathen in our sight<br>_By_ the revenging<sup>b</sup> of the blood of thy servants _which is_ shed.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Let the sighing of the prisoner come before thee;<br>According to the greatness of thy power<sup>c</sup> preserve thou those that are appointed to die;<sup>d</sup><br>
<sup>12</sup> And render unto our neighbours sevenfold into their bosom<br>Their reproach, wherewith they have reproached thee, O Lord.<br>
<sup>13</sup> So we thy people and sheep of thy pasture will give thee thanks for ever:<br>We will shew forth thy praise to all generations.<sup>e</sup><br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 8. Or, _the iniquities of them that were before us_
> - <sup>b</sup> 10. Heb. _vengeance_
> - <sup>c</sup> 11. Heb. _thine arm_
> - <sup>d</sup> 11. Heb. _reserve the children of death_
> - <sup>e</sup> 13. Heb. _to generation and generation_

## Chapter 80

> _To the chief Musician upon Shoshannim-eduth, A Psalm of Asaph._

<sup>1</sup> Give ear, O Shepherd of Israel, thou that leadest Joseph like a flock;<br>Thou that dwellest _between_ the cherubims, shine forth.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Before Ephraim and Benjamin and Manasseh stir up thy strength,<br>And come _and_ save us.<sup>a</sup><br>
<sup>3</sup> Turn us again, O God,<br>And cause thy face to shine; and we shall be saved.<br>
<sup>4</sup> O LORD God of hosts,<br>How long wilt thou be angry<sup>b</sup><sup>c</sup> against the prayer of thy people?<br>
<sup>5</sup> Thou feedest them with the bread of tears;<br>And givest them tears to drink in great measure.<br>
<sup>6</sup> Thou makest us a strife unto our neighbours:<br>And our enemies laugh among themselves.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Turn us again, O God of hosts,<br>And cause thy face to shine; and we shall be saved.<br>
<sup>8</sup> Thou hast brought a vine out of Egypt:<br>Thou hast cast out the heathen, and planted it.<br>
<sup>9</sup> Thou preparedst _room_ before it,<br>And didst cause it to take deep root, and it filled the land.<br>
<sup>10</sup> The hills were covered with the shadow of it,<br>And the boughs thereof _were like_ the goodly cedars.<sup>d</sup><br>
<sup>11</sup> She sent out her boughs unto the sea,<br>And her branches unto the river.<br>
<sup>12</sup> Why hast thou _then_ broken down her hedges,<br>So that all they which pass by the way do pluck her?<br>
<sup>13</sup> The boar out of the wood doth waste it,<br>And the wild beast of the field doth devour it.<br>
<sup>14</sup> Return, we beseech thee, O God of hosts: Look down from heaven,<br>And behold, and visit this vine;<br>
<sup>15</sup> And the vineyard which thy right hand hath planted,<br>And the branch _that_ thou madest strong for thyself.<br>
<sup>16</sup> _It is_ burned with fire, _it is_ cut down:<br>They perish at the rebuke of thy countenance.<br>
<sup>17</sup> Let thy hand be upon the man of thy right hand,<br>Upon the son of man _whom_ thou madest strong for thyself.<br>
<sup>18</sup> So will not we go back from thee:<br>Quicken us, and we will call upon thy name.<br>
<sup>19</sup> Turn us again, O LORD God of hosts,<br>Cause thy face to shine; and we shall be saved.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 2. Heb. _come for salvation to us_
> - <sup>b</sup> 4. Heb. _wilt thou smoke?_
> - <sup>c</sup> 4. Heb. _wilt thou smoke?_
> - <sup>d</sup> 10. Heb. _the Cedars of God_

## Chapter 81

> _To the chief Musician upon Gittith, _A Psalm_ of Asaph._

<sup>1</sup> Sing aloud unto God our strength:<br>Make a joyful noise unto the God of Jacob.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Take a psalm, and bring hither the timbrel,<br>The pleasant harp with the psaltery.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Blow up the trumpet in the new moon,<br>In the time appointed, on our solemn feast day.<br>
<sup>4</sup> For this _was_ a statute for Israel,<br>_And_ a law of the God of Jacob.<br>
<sup>5</sup> This he ordained in Joseph _for_ a testimony,<br>When he went out through<sup>a</sup> the land of Egypt:<br>_Where_ I heard a language _that_ I understood not.<br>
<sup>6</sup> I removed his shoulder from the burden:<br>His hands were delivered<sup>b</sup> from the pots.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Thou calledst in trouble, and I delivered thee;<br>I answered thee in the secret place of thunder:<br>I proved thee<br>At the waters of Meribah.<sup>c</sup> _Selah._<br>
<sup>8</sup> Hear, O my people, and I will testify unto thee:<br>O Israel, if thou wilt hearken unto me;<br>
<sup>9</sup> There shall no strange god be in thee;<br>Neither shalt thou worship any strange god.<br>
<sup>10</sup> I _am_ the LORD thy God, which brought thee out of the land of Egypt:<br>Open thy mouth wide, and I will fill it.<br>
<sup>11</sup> But my people would not hearken to my voice;<br>And Israel would none of me.<br>
<sup>12</sup> So I gave them up unto their own hearts’ lust:<br>_And_ they walked in their own counsels.<br>
<sup>13</sup> Oh that my people had hearkened unto me,<br>_And_ Israel had walked in my ways!<br>
<sup>14</sup> I should soon have subdued their enemies,<br>And turned my hand against their adversaries.<br>
<sup>15</sup> The haters of the LORD should have submitted themselves<sup>d</sup> unto him:<br>But their time should have endured for ever.<br>
<sup>16</sup> He should have fed them also with the finest of the wheat:<sup>e</sup><br>And with honey out of the rock should I have satisfied thee.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 5. Or, _against_
> - <sup>b</sup> 6. Heb. _passed away_
> - <sup>c</sup> 7. Or, _strife_
> - <sup>d</sup> 15. Or, _yielded feigned obedience, Heb. lied_
> - <sup>e</sup> 16. Heb. _with the fat of wheat_

## Chapter 82

> _A Psalm of Asaph._

<sup>1</sup> God standeth in the congregation of the mighty;<br>He judgeth among the gods.<br>
<sup>2</sup> How long will ye judge unjustly,<br>And accept the persons of the wicked? _Selah._<br>
<sup>3</sup> Defend<sup>a</sup> the poor and fatherless:<br>Do justice to the afflicted and needy.<br>
<sup>4</sup> Deliver the poor and needy:<br>Rid _them_ out of the hand of the wicked.<br>
<sup>5</sup> They know not, neither will they understand; They walk on in darkness:<br>All the foundations of the earth are out of course.<sup>b</sup><br>
<sup>6</sup> I have said, Ye _are_ gods;<br>And all of you _are_ children of the most High.<br>
<sup>7</sup> But ye shall die like men,<br>And fall like one of the princes.<br>
<sup>8</sup> Arise, O God, judge the earth:<br>For thou shalt inherit all nations.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 3. Heb. _judge_
> - <sup>b</sup> 5. Heb. _moved_

## Chapter 83

> _A Song _or_ Psalm of Asaph._

<sup>1</sup> Keep not thou silence, O God:<br>Hold not thy peace, and be not still, O God.<br>
<sup>2</sup> For, lo, thine enemies make a tumult:<br>And they that hate thee have lifted up the head.<br>
<sup>3</sup> They have taken crafty counsel against thy people,<br>And consulted against thy hidden ones.<br>
<sup>4</sup> They have said, Come, and let us cut them off from _being_ a nation;<br>That the name of Israel may be no more in remembrance.<br>
<sup>5</sup> For they have consulted together with one consent:<sup>a</sup><br>They are confederate against thee:<br>
<sup>6</sup> The tabernacles of Edom, and the Ishmaelites;<br>Of Moab, and the Hagarenes;<br>
<sup>7</sup> Gebal, and Ammon, and Amalek;<br>The Philistines with the inhabitants of Tyre;<br>
<sup>8</sup> Assur also is joined with them:<br>They have holpen the children of Lot.<sup>b</sup> _Selah._<br>
<sup>9</sup> Do unto them as _unto_ the Midianites;<br>As _to_ Sisera, as _to_ Jabin, at the brook of Kison:<br>
<sup>10</sup> _Which_ perished at Endor:<br>They became _as_ dung for the earth.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Make their nobles like Oreb, and like Zeeb:<br>Yea, all their princes as Zebah, and as Zalmunna:<br>
<sup>12</sup> Who said, Let us take to ourselves<br>The houses of God in possession.<br>
<sup>13</sup> O my God, make them like a wheel;<br>As the stubble before the wind.<br>
<sup>14</sup> As the fire burneth a wood,<br>And as the flame setteth the mountains on fire;<br>
<sup>15</sup> So persecute them with thy tempest,<br>And make them afraid with thy storm.<br>
<sup>16</sup> Fill their faces with shame;<br>That they may seek thy name, O LORD.<br>
<sup>17</sup> Let them be confounded and troubled for ever;<br>Yea, let them be put to shame, and perish:<br>
<sup>18</sup> That _men_ may know that thou, whose name alone _is_ JEHOVAH,<br>_Art_ the Most High over all the earth.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 5. Heb. _heart_
> - <sup>b</sup> 8. Heb. _they have been an arm to the children of Lot_

## Chapter 84

> _To the chief Musician upon Gittith, A Psalm for the sons of Korah._

<sup>1</sup> How amiable _are_ thy tabernacles, O LORD of hosts!<br>
<sup>2</sup> My soul longeth, yea, even fainteth for the courts of the LORD:<br>My heart and my flesh crieth out for the living God.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Yea, the sparrow hath found an house, and the swallow a nest for herself, where she may lay her young,<br>_Even_ thine altars, O LORD of hosts, my King, and my God.<br>
<sup>4</sup> Blessed _are_ they that dwell in thy house:<br>They will be still praising thee. _Selah._<br>
<sup>5</sup> Blessed _is_ the man whose strength _is_<br>in thee; In whose heart _are_ the ways _of them_.<br>
<sup>6</sup> _Who_ passing through the valley of Baca make it a well;<br>The rain also filleth<sup>a</sup> the pools.<br>
<sup>7</sup> They go from strength to strength,<sup>b</sup><br>_Every one of them_ in Zion appeareth before God.<br>
<sup>8</sup> O LORD God of hosts, hear my prayer:<br>Give ear, O God of Jacob. _Selah._<br>
<sup>9</sup> Behold, O God our shield,<br>And look upon the face of thine anointed.<br>
<sup>10</sup> For a day in thy courts<br>_Is_ better than a thousand.<br>I had rather be a doorkeeper<sup>c</sup> in the house of my God,<br>Than to dwell in the tents of wickedness.<br>
<sup>11</sup> For the LORD God _is_ a sun and shield:<br>The LORD will give grace and glory: No good _thing_ will he withhold from them that walk uprightly.<br>
<sup>12</sup> O LORD of hosts,<br>Blessed _is_ the man that trusteth in thee.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 6. Heb. _covereth_
> - <sup>b</sup> 7. Or, _from company to company_
> - <sup>c</sup> 10. Heb. _I would choose rather to sit at the threshhold_

## Chapter 85

> _To the chief Musician, A Psalm for the sons of Korah._

<sup>1</sup> LORD, thou hast been favourable unto thy land:<br>Thou hast brought back the captivity of Jacob.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Thou hast forgiven the iniquity of thy people,<br>Thou hast covered all their sin. _Selah._<br>
<sup>3</sup> Thou hast taken away all thy wrath:<br>Thou hast turned _thyself_ from the fierceness of thine anger.<sup>a</sup><br>
<sup>4</sup> Turn us, O God of our salvation,<br>And cause thine anger toward us to cease.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Wilt thou be angry with us for ever?<br>Wilt thou draw out thine anger to all generations?<br>
<sup>6</sup> Wilt thou not revive us again:<br>That thy people may rejoice in thee?<br>
<sup>7</sup> Shew us thy mercy, O LORD,<br>And grant us thy salvation.<br>
<sup>8</sup> I will hear what God the LORD will speak:<br>For he will speak peace unto his people, and to his saints: But let them not turn again to folly.<br>
<sup>9</sup> Surely his salvation _is_ nigh them that fear him;<br>That glory may dwell in our land.<br>
<sup>10</sup> Mercy and truth are met together;<br>Righteousness and peace have kissed _each other_.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Truth shall spring out of the earth;<br>And righteousness shall look down from heaven.<br>
<sup>12</sup> Yea, the LORD shall give _that which is_ good;<br>And our land shall yield her increase.<br>
<sup>13</sup> Righteousness shall go before him;<br>And shall set _us_ in the way of his steps.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 3. Or, _thou hast turned thine anger from waxing hot_

## Chapter 86

> _A Prayer of David._

<sup>1</sup> Bow down thine ear, O LORD, hear me:<br>For I _am_ poor and needy.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Preserve my soul; for I _am_ holy:<sup>a</sup><br>O thou my God, save thy servant that trusteth in thee.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Be merciful unto me, O Lord:<br>For I cry unto thee daily.<sup>b</sup><br>
<sup>4</sup> Rejoice the soul of thy servant:<br>For unto thee, O Lord, do I lift up my soul.<br>
<sup>5</sup> For thou, Lord, _art_ good, and ready to forgive;<br>And plenteous in mercy unto all them that call upon thee.<br>
<sup>6</sup> Give ear, O LORD, unto my prayer;<br>And attend to the voice of my supplications.<br>
<sup>7</sup> In the day of my trouble I will call upon thee:<br>For thou wilt answer me.<br>
<sup>8</sup> Among the gods _there is_ none like unto thee, O Lord;<br>Neither _are there any works_ like unto thy works.<br>
<sup>9</sup> All nations whom thou hast made shall come and worship before thee, O Lord;<br>And shall glorify thy name.<br>
<sup>10</sup> For thou _art_ great, and doest wondrous things:<br>Thou _art_ God alone.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Teach me thy way, O LORD; I will walk in thy truth:<br>Unite my heart to fear thy name.<br>
<sup>12</sup> I will praise thee, O Lord my God, with all my heart:<br>And I will glorify thy name for evermore.<br>
<sup>13</sup> For great _is_ thy mercy toward me:<br>And thou hast delivered my soul from the lowest hell.<sup>c</sup><br>
<sup>14</sup> O God, the proud are risen against me,<br>And the assemblies of violent<sup>d</sup> _men_ have sought after my soul; And have not set thee before them.<br>
<sup>15</sup> But thou, O Lord, _art_ a God full of compassion, and gracious,<br>Longsuffering, and plenteous in mercy and truth.<br>
<sup>16</sup> O turn unto me, and have mercy upon me;<br>Give thy strength unto thy servant, and save the son of thine handmaid.<br>
<sup>17</sup> Shew me a token for good; That they which hate me may see _it_, and be ashamed:<br>Because thou, LORD, hast holpen me, and comforted me.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 2. Or, _one whom thou favourest_
> - <sup>b</sup> 3. Or, _all the day_
> - <sup>c</sup> 13. Or, _grave_
> - <sup>d</sup> 14. Heb. _terrible_

## Chapter 87

> _A Psalm _or_ Song for the sons of Korah._

<sup>1</sup> His foundation _is_ in the holy mountains.<br>
<sup>2</sup> The LORD loveth the gates of Zion more than all the dwellings of Jacob.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Glorious things are spoken of thee,<br>O city of God. _Selah._<br>
<sup>4</sup> I will make mention of Rahab and Babylon to them that know me:<br>Behold Philistia, and Tyre, with Ethiopia;<br>This _man_ was born there.<br>
<sup>5</sup> And of Zion it shall be said, This and that man was born in her:<br>And the highest himself shall establish her.<br>
<sup>6</sup> The LORD shall count, when he writeth up the people,<br>_That_ this _man_ was born there. _Selah._<br>
<sup>7</sup> As well the singers as the players on instruments _shall be there:_<br>All my springs _are_ in thee.<br>

## Chapter 88

> _A Song _or_ Psalm for the sons of Korah, to the chief Musician upon Mahalath Leannoth, Maschil of Heman the Ezrahite._

<sup>1</sup> O LORD God of my salvation, I have cried day _and_ night before thee:<br>
<sup>2</sup> Let my prayer come before thee: Incline thine ear unto my cry;<br>
<sup>3</sup> For my soul is full of troubles:<br>And my life draweth nigh unto the grave.<br>
<sup>4</sup> I am counted with them that go down into the pit:<br>I am as a man _that hath_ no strength:<br>
<sup>5</sup> Free among the dead, like the slain that lie in the grave,<br>Whom thou rememberest no more: And they are cut off from thy hand.<sup>a</sup><br>
<sup>6</sup> Thou hast laid me in the lowest pit,<br>In darkness, in the deeps.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Thy wrath lieth hard upon me,<br>And thou hast afflicted _me_ with all thy waves. _Selah._<br>
<sup>8</sup> Thou hast put away mine acquaintance far from me;<br>Thou hast made me an abomination unto them:<br>_I am_ shut up,<br>And I cannot come forth.<br>
<sup>9</sup> Mine eye mourneth by reason of affliction:<br>LORD, I have called daily upon thee, I have stretched out my hands unto thee.<br>
<sup>10</sup> Wilt thou shew wonders to the dead?<br>Shall the dead arise _and_ praise thee? _Selah._<br>
<sup>11</sup> Shall thy lovingkindness be declared in the grave?<br>_Or_ thy faithfulness in destruction?<br>
<sup>12</sup> Shall thy wonders be known in the dark?<br>And thy righteousness in the land of forgetfulness?<br>
<sup>13</sup> But unto thee have I cried, O LORD;<br>And in the morning shall my prayer prevent thee.<br>
<sup>14</sup> LORD, why castest thou off my soul?<br>_Why_ hidest thou thy face from me?<br>
<sup>15</sup> I _am_ afflicted and ready to die<br>From _my_ youth up: _While_ I suffer thy terrors I am distracted.<br>
<sup>16</sup> Thy fierce wrath goeth over me;<br>Thy terrors have cut me off.<br>
<sup>17</sup> They came round about me daily<sup>b</sup> like water;<br>They compassed me about together.<br>
<sup>18</sup> Lover and friend hast thou put far from me,<br>_And_ mine acquaintance into darkness.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 5. Or, _by thy hand_
> - <sup>b</sup> 17. Or, _all the day_

## Chapter 89

> _Maschil of Ethan the Ezrahite._

<sup>1</sup> I will sing of the mercies of the LORD for ever:<br>With my mouth will I make known thy faithfulness to all generations.<sup>a</sup><br>
<sup>2</sup> For I have said, Mercy shall be built up for ever:<br>Thy faithfulness shalt thou establish in the very heavens.<br>
<sup>3</sup> I have made a covenant with my chosen,<br>I have sworn unto David my servant,<br>
<sup>4</sup> Thy seed will I establish for ever,<br>And build up thy throne to all generations.<sup>b</sup> _Selah._<br>
<sup>5</sup> And the heavens shall praise thy wonders, O LORD:<br>Thy faithfulness also in the congregation of the saints.<br>
<sup>6</sup> For who in the heaven<br>Can be compared unto the LORD?<br>_Who_ among the sons of the mighty<br>Can be likened unto the LORD?<br>
<sup>7</sup> God is greatly to be feared in the assembly of the saints,<br>And to be had in reverence of all _them that are_ about him.<br>
<sup>8</sup> O LORD God of hosts, who _is_ a strong LORD like unto thee?<br>Or to thy faithfulness round about thee?<br>
<sup>9</sup> Thou rulest the raging of the sea:<br>When the waves thereof arise, thou stillest them.<br>
<sup>10</sup> Thou hast broken Rahab<sup>c</sup> in pieces, as one that is slain;<br>Thou hast scattered thine enemies with thy strong arm.<sup>d</sup><br>
<sup>11</sup> The heavens _are_ thine, the earth also _is_ thine:<br>_As for_ the world and the fulness thereof, thou hast founded them.<br>
<sup>12</sup> The north and the south thou hast created them:<br>Tabor and Hermon shall rejoice in thy name.<br>
<sup>13</sup> Thou hast a mighty arm:<sup>e</sup><br>Strong is thy hand, _and_ high is thy right hand.<br>
<sup>14</sup> Justice and judgment _are_ the habitation<sup>f</sup> of thy throne:<br>Mercy and truth shall go before thy face.<br>
<sup>15</sup> Blessed _is_ the people that know the joyful sound:<br>They shall walk, O LORD, in the light of thy countenance.<br>
<sup>16</sup> In thy name shall they rejoice all the day:<br>And in thy righteousness shall they be exalted.<br>
<sup>17</sup> For thou _art_ the glory of their strength:<br>And in thy favour our horn shall be exalted.<br>
<sup>18</sup> For the LORD _is_ our defence;<br>And the Holy One of Israel _is_ our King.<br>
<sup>19</sup> Then thou spakest in vision to thy holy one, and saidst,<br>I have laid help upon _one that is_ mighty; I have exalted _one_ chosen out of the people.<br>
<sup>20</sup> I have found David my servant;<br>With my holy oil have I anointed him:<br>
<sup>21</sup> With whom my hand shall be established:<br>Mine arm also shall strengthen him.<br>
<sup>22</sup> The enemy shall not exact upon him;<br>Nor the son of wickedness afflict him.<br>
<sup>23</sup> And I will beat down his foes before his face,<br>And plague them that hate him.<br>
<sup>24</sup> But my faithfulness and my mercy _shall be_ with him:<br>And in my name shall his horn be exalted.<br>
<sup>25</sup> I will set his hand also in the sea,<br>And his right hand in the rivers.<br>
<sup>26</sup> He shall cry unto me, Thou _art_ my father,<br>My God, and the rock of my salvation.<br>
<sup>27</sup> Also I will make him _my_ firstborn,<br>Higher than the kings of the earth.<br>
<sup>28</sup> My mercy will I keep for him for evermore,<br>And my covenant shall stand fast with him.<br>
<sup>29</sup> His seed also will I make _to endure_ for ever,<br>And his throne as the days of heaven.<br>
<sup>30</sup> If his children forsake my law,<br>And walk not in my judgments;<br>
<sup>31</sup> If they break my statutes,<sup>g</sup> and keep not my commandments;<br>
<sup>32</sup> Then will I visit their transgression with the rod, and their iniquity with stripes.<br>
<sup>33</sup> Nevertheless my lovingkindness will I not utterly take from him,<br>Nor suffer my faithfulness to fail.<sup>h</sup><br>
<sup>34</sup> My covenant will I not break, nor alter the thing that is gone out of my lips.<br>
<sup>35</sup> Once have I sworn by my holiness that I will not lie<sup>i</sup> unto David.<br>
<sup>36</sup> His seed shall endure for ever,<br>And his throne as the sun before me.<br>
<sup>37</sup> It shall be established for ever as the moon,<br>And _as_ a faithful witness in heaven. _Selah._<br>
<sup>38</sup> But thou hast cast off and abhorred,<br>Thou hast been wroth with thine anointed.<br>
<sup>39</sup> Thou hast made void the covenant of thy servant:<br>Thou hast profaned his crown _by casting it_ to the ground.<br>
<sup>40</sup> Thou hast broken down all his hedges;<br>Thou hast brought his strong holds to ruin.<br>
<sup>41</sup> All that pass by the way spoil him:<br>He is a reproach to his neighbours.<br>
<sup>42</sup> Thou hast set up the right hand of his adversaries;<br>Thou hast made all his enemies to rejoice.<br>
<sup>43</sup> Thou hast also turned the edge of his sword,<br>And hast not made him to stand in the battle.<br>
<sup>44</sup> Thou hast made his glory<sup>j</sup> to cease,<br>And cast his throne down to the ground.<br>
<sup>45</sup> The days of his youth hast thou shortened:<br>Thou hast covered him with shame. _Selah._<br>
<sup>46</sup> How long, LORD? wilt thou hide thyself for ever?<br>Shall thy wrath burn like fire?<br>
<sup>47</sup> Remember how short my time is:<br>Wherefore hast thou made all men in vain?<br>
<sup>48</sup> What man _is he that_ liveth, and shall not see death?<br>Shall he deliver his soul from the hand of the grave? _Selah._<br>
<sup>49</sup> Lord, where _are_ thy former lovingkindnesses,<br>_Which_ thou swarest unto David in thy truth?<br>
<sup>50</sup> Remember, Lord, the reproach of thy servants;<br>_How_ I do bear in my bosom _the reproach of_ all the mighty people;<br>
<sup>51</sup> Wherewith thine enemies have reproached, O LORD;<br>Wherewith they have reproached the footsteps of thine anointed.<br>
<sup>52</sup> Blessed _be_ the LORD for evermore.<br>Amen, and Amen.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Heb. _to generation and generation_
> - <sup>b</sup> 4. Heb. _to generation and generation_
> - <sup>c</sup> 10. Or, _Egypt_
> - <sup>d</sup> 10. Heb. _with the arm of thy strength_
> - <sup>e</sup> 13. Heb. _an arm with might_
> - <sup>f</sup> 14. Or, _establishment_
> - <sup>g</sup> 31. Heb. _profane my statutes_
> - <sup>h</sup> 33. Heb. _to lie_
> - <sup>i</sup> 35. _if I lie_
> - <sup>j</sup> 44. Heb. _brightness_

## Chapter 90

> _A Prayer of Moses the man of God._

<sup>1</sup> LORD, thou hast been our dwelling place<br>In all generations.<sup>a</sup><br>
<sup>2</sup> Before the mountains were brought forth, or ever thou hadst formed the earth and the world,<br>Even from everlasting to everlasting, thou _art_ God.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Thou turnest man to destruction;<br>And sayest, Return, ye children of men.<br>
<sup>4</sup> For a thousand years in thy sight _are but_ as yesterday<br>When it is past,<sup>b</sup> and _as_ a watch in the night.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Thou carriest them away as with a flood; they are _as_ a sleep:<br>In the morning _they are_ like grass _which_ groweth up.<sup>c</sup><br>
<sup>6</sup> In the morning it flourisheth, and groweth up;<br>In the evening it is cut down, and withereth.<br>
<sup>7</sup> For we are consumed by thine anger,<br>And by thy wrath are we troubled.<br>
<sup>8</sup> Thou hast set our iniquities before thee,<br>Our secret _sins_ in the light of thy countenance.<br>
<sup>9</sup> For all our days are passed away<sup>d</sup> in thy wrath:<br>We spend our years as a tale<sup>e</sup> _that is told_.<br>
<sup>10</sup> The days of our years _are_ threescore years and ten;<sup>f</sup> And if by reason of strength _they be_ fourscore years,<br>Yet _is_ their strength labour and sorrow; For it is soon cut off, and we fly away.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Who knoweth the power of thine anger?<br>Even according to thy fear, _so is_ thy wrath.<br>
<sup>12</sup> So teach _us_ to number our days,<br>That we may apply<sup>g</sup> _our_ hearts unto wisdom.<br>
<sup>13</sup> Return, O LORD, how long?<br>And let it repent thee concerning thy servants.<br>
<sup>14</sup> O satisfy us early with thy mercy;<br>That we may rejoice and be glad all our days.<br>
<sup>15</sup> Make us glad according to the days _wherein_ thou hast afflicted us,<br>_And_ the years _wherein_ we have seen evil.<br>
<sup>16</sup> Let thy work appear unto thy servants,<br>And thy glory unto their children.<br>
<sup>17</sup> And let the beauty of the LORD our God be upon us:<br>And establish thou the work of our hands upon us; Yea, the work of our hands establish thou it.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Heb. _in generation and generation_
> - <sup>b</sup> 4. Or, _when he hath passed them_
> - <sup>c</sup> 5. Or, _is changed_
> - <sup>d</sup> 9. Heb. _turned away_
> - <sup>e</sup> 9. Or, _as a meditation_
> - <sup>f</sup> 10. Heb. _As for the days of our years, in them are seventy years_
> - <sup>g</sup> 12. Heb. _cause to come_

## Chapter 91

<sup>1</sup> He that dwelleth in the secret place of the Most High<br>Shall abide<sup>a</sup> under the shadow of the Almighty.<br>
<sup>2</sup> I will say of the LORD, _He is_ my refuge and my fortress:<br>My God; in him will I trust.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Surely he shall deliver thee from the snare of the fowler,<br>_And_ from the noisome pestilence.<br>
<sup>4</sup> He shall cover thee with his feathers, and under his wings shalt thou trust:<br>His truth _shall be thy_ shield and buckler.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Thou shalt not be afraid for the terror by night;<br>_Nor_ for the arrow _that_ flieth by day;<br>
<sup>6</sup> _Nor_ for the pestilence _that_ walketh in darkness;<br>_Nor_ for the destruction _that_ wasteth at noonday.<br>
<sup>7</sup> A thousand shall fall at thy side, and ten thousand at thy right hand;<br>_But_ it shall not come nigh thee.<br>
<sup>8</sup> Only with thine eyes shalt thou behold<br>And see the reward of the wicked.<br>
<sup>9</sup> Because thou hast made the LORD, _which is_ my refuge,<br>_Even_ the most High, thy habitation;<br>
<sup>10</sup> There shall no evil befall thee,<br>Neither shall any plague come nigh thy dwelling.<br>
<sup>11</sup> For he shall give his angels charge over thee,<br>To keep thee in all thy ways.<br>
<sup>12</sup> They shall bear thee up in _their_ hands,<br>Lest thou dash thy foot against a stone.<br>
<sup>13</sup> Thou shalt tread upon the lion and adder:<sup>b</sup><br>The young lion and the dragon shalt thou trample under feet.<br>
<sup>14</sup> Because he hath set his love upon me, therefore will I deliver him:<br>I will set him on high, because he hath known my name.<br>
<sup>15</sup> He shall call upon me, and I will answer him:<br>I _will be_ with him in trouble; I will deliver him, and honour him.<br>
<sup>16</sup> With long life<sup>c</sup> will I satisfy him,<br>And shew him my salvation.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Heb. _lodge_
> - <sup>b</sup> 13. Or, _asp_
> - <sup>c</sup> 16. Heb. _length of days_

## Chapter 92

> _A Psalm _or_ Song for the sabbath day._

<sup>1</sup> _It is_ a good thing to give thanks unto the LORD,<br>And to sing praises unto thy name, O most High:<br>
<sup>2</sup> To shew forth thy lovingkindness in the morning,<br>And thy faithfulness every night,<sup>a</sup><br>
<sup>3</sup> Upon an instrument of ten strings, and upon the psaltery;<br>Upon the harp with a solemn sound.<sup>b</sup><br>
<sup>4</sup> For thou, LORD, hast made me glad through thy work:<br>I will triumph in the works of thy hands.<br>
<sup>5</sup> O LORD, how great are thy works!<br>_And_ thy thoughts are very deep.<br>
<sup>6</sup> A brutish man knoweth not;<br>Neither doth a fool understand this.<br>
<sup>7</sup> When the wicked spring as the grass,<br>And when all the workers of iniquity do flourish;<br>_It is_ that they shall be destroyed for ever:<br>
<sup>8</sup> But thou, LORD, _art most_ high for evermore.<br>
<sup>9</sup> For, lo, thine enemies, O LORD, for, lo, thine enemies shall perish;<br>All the workers of iniquity shall be scattered.<br>
<sup>10</sup> But my horn shalt thou exalt like _the horn of_ an unicorn:<br>I shall be anointed with fresh oil.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Mine eye also shall see _my desire_ on mine enemies,<br>_And_ mine ears shall hear _my desire_ of the wicked that rise up against me.<br>
<sup>12</sup> The righteous shall flourish like the palm tree:<br>He shall grow like a cedar in Lebanon.<br>
<sup>13</sup> Those that be planted in the house of the LORD<br>Shall flourish in the courts of our God.<br>
<sup>14</sup> They shall still bring forth fruit in old age;<br>They shall be fat and flourishing;<sup>c</sup><br>
<sup>15</sup> To shew that the LORD _is_ upright: _He is_ my rock,<br>And _there is_ no unrighteousness in him.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 2. Heb. _in the nights_
> - <sup>b</sup> 3. Or, _upon the solemn sound with the harp_. Heb. _Higgaion_
> - <sup>c</sup> 14. Heb. _green_

## Chapter 93

<sup>1</sup> The LORD reigneth, he is clothed with majesty;<br>The LORD is clothed with strength, _wherewith_ he hath girded himself:<br>The world also is stablished,<br>That it cannot be moved.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Thy throne _is_ established of old:<sup>a</sup><br>Thou _art_ from everlasting.<br>
<sup>3</sup> The floods have lifted up, O LORD, the floods have lifted up their voice;<br>The floods lift up their waves.<br>
<sup>4</sup> The LORD on high _is_ mightier than the noise of many waters,<br>_Yea, than_ the mighty waves of the sea.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Thy testimonies are very sure:<br>Holiness becometh thine house, O LORD, for ever.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 2. Heb. _from then_

## Chapter 94

<sup>1</sup> O LORD God, to whom vengeance belongeth;<sup>a</sup><br>O God, to whom vengeance belongeth, shew<sup>b</sup> thyself.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Lift up thyself, thou judge of the earth:<br>Render a reward to the proud.<br>
<sup>3</sup> LORD, how long shall the wicked,<br>How long shall the wicked triumph?<br>
<sup>4</sup> _How long_ shall they utter _and_ speak hard things?<br>_And_ all the workers of iniquity boast themselves?<br>
<sup>5</sup> They break in pieces thy people, O LORD,<br>And afflict thine heritage.<br>
<sup>6</sup> They slay the widow and the stranger,<br>And murder the fatherless.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Yet they say, The LORD shall not see,<br>Neither shall the God of Jacob regard _it_.<br>
<sup>8</sup> Understand, ye brutish among the people:<br>And _ye_ fools, when will ye be wise?<br>
<sup>9</sup> He that planted the ear, shall he not hear?<br>He that formed the eye, shall he not see?<br>
<sup>10</sup> He that chastiseth the heathen, shall not he correct?<br>He that teacheth man knowledge, _shall not he know?_<br>
<sup>11</sup> The LORD knoweth the thoughts of man,<br>That they _are_ vanity.<br>
<sup>12</sup> Blessed _is_ the man whom thou chastenest, O LORD,<br>And teachest him out of thy law;<br>
<sup>13</sup> That thou mayest give him rest from the days of adversity,<br>Until the pit be digged for the wicked.<br>
<sup>14</sup> For the LORD will not cast off his people,<br>Neither will he forsake his inheritance.<br>
<sup>15</sup> But judgment shall return unto righteousness:<br>And all the upright in heart shall follow it.<sup>c</sup><br>
<sup>16</sup> Who will rise up for me against the evildoers?<br>_Or_ who will stand up for me against the workers of iniquity?<br>
<sup>17</sup> Unless the LORD _had been_ my help,<br>My soul had almost<sup>d</sup> dwelt in silence.<br>
<sup>18</sup> When I said, My foot slippeth;<br>Thy mercy, O LORD, held me up.<br>
<sup>19</sup> In the multitude of my thoughts within me<br>Thy comforts delight my soul.<br>
<sup>20</sup> Shall the throne of iniquity have fellowship with thee,<br>Which frameth mischief by a law?<br>
<sup>21</sup> They gather themselves together against the soul of the righteous,<br>And condemn the innocent blood.<br>
<sup>22</sup> But the LORD is my defence;<br>And my God _is_ the rock of my refuge.<br>
<sup>23</sup> And he shall bring upon them their own iniquity, and shall cut them off in their own wickedness;<br>_Yea_, the LORD our God shall cut them off.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Heb. _God of revenges_
> - <sup>b</sup> 1. Heb. _shine forth_
> - <sup>c</sup> 15. Heb. _shall be after it_
> - <sup>d</sup> 17. Or, _quickly_

## Chapter 95

<sup>1</sup> O come, let us sing unto the LORD:<br>Let us make a joyful noise to the rock of our salvation.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Let us come before his presence<sup>a</sup> with thanksgiving,<br>And make a joyful noise unto him with psalms.<br>
<sup>3</sup> For the LORD _is_ a great God,<br>And a great King above all gods.<br>
<sup>4</sup> In his<sup>b</sup> hand _are_ the deep places of the earth:<br>The strength of the hills _is_ his also.<sup>c</sup><br>
<sup>5</sup> The sea _is_ his,<sup>d</sup> and he made it:<br>And his hands formed the dry _land_.<br>
<sup>6</sup> O come, let us worship and bow down:<br>Let us kneel before the LORD our maker.<br>
<sup>7</sup> For he _is_ our God;<br>And we _are_ the people of his pasture, and the sheep of his hand.<br>To day if ye will hear his voice,
<sup>8</sup> Harden not your heart,<br>As in the provocation,<sup>e</sup> _and_ as _in_ the day of temptation in the wilderness:<br>
<sup>9</sup> When your fathers tempted me,<br>Proved me, and saw my work.<br>
<sup>10</sup> Forty years long was I grieved with _this_ generation,<br>And said, It _is_ a people that do err in their heart, and they have not known my ways:<br>
<sup>11</sup> Unto whom I sware in my wrath<br>That they should not enter into my rest.<sup>f</sup><br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 2. Heb. _prevent his face_
> - <sup>b</sup> 4. Heb. _in whose_
> - <sup>c</sup> 4. Or, _the heights of the hills, are his_
> - <sup>d</sup> 5. Heb. _Whose the sea is_
> - <sup>e</sup> 8. Heb. _contention_
> - <sup>f</sup> 11. Heb. _if they enter into my rest_

## Chapter 96

<sup>1</sup> O Sing unto the LORD a new song:<br>Sing unto the LORD, all the earth.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Sing unto the LORD, bless his name;<br>Shew forth his salvation from day to day.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Declare his glory among the heathen,<br>His wonders among all people.<br>
<sup>4</sup> For the LORD _is_ great, and greatly to be praised:<br>He _is_ to be feared above all gods.<br>
<sup>5</sup> For all the gods of the nations _are_ idols:<br>But the LORD made the heavens.<br>
<sup>6</sup> Honour and majesty _are_ before him:<br>Strength and beauty _are_ in his sanctuary.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Give unto the LORD, O ye kindreds of the people,<br>Give unto the LORD glory and strength.<br>
<sup>8</sup> Give unto the LORD the glory _due unto_ his name:<sup>a</sup><br>Bring an offering, and come into his courts.<br>
<sup>9</sup> O worship the LORD in the beauty of holiness:<sup>b</sup><br>Fear before him, all the earth.<br>
<sup>10</sup> Say among the heathen _that_ the LORD reigneth:<br>The world also shall be established that it shall not be moved: He shall judge the people righteously.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Let the heavens rejoice, and let the earth be glad;<br>Let the sea roar, and the fulness thereof.<br>
<sup>12</sup> Let the field be joyful, and all that _is_ therein:<br>Then shall all the trees of the wood rejoice<br>
<sup>13</sup> Before the LORD: for he cometh, for he cometh to judge the earth:<br>He shall judge the world with righteousness, and the people with his truth.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 8. Heb. _of his name_
> - <sup>b</sup> 9. Or, _in the glorious sanctuary_

## Chapter 97

<sup>1</sup> The LORD reigneth; let the earth rejoice;<br>Let the multitude of isles<sup>a</sup> be glad _thereof_.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Clouds and darkness _are_ round about him:<br>Righteousness and judgment _are_ the habitation<sup>b</sup> of his throne.<br>
<sup>3</sup> A fire goeth before him,<br>And burneth up his enemies round about.<br>
<sup>4</sup> His lightnings enlightened the world:<br>The earth saw, and trembled.<br>
<sup>5</sup> The hills melted like wax at the presence of the LORD,<br>At the presence of the Lord of the whole earth.<br>
<sup>6</sup> The heavens declare his righteousness,<br>And all the people see his glory.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Confounded be all they that serve graven images,<br>That boast themselves of idols: Worship him, all _ye_ gods.<br>
<sup>8</sup> Zion heard, and was glad;<br>And the daughters of Judah rejoiced because of thy judgments, O LORD.<br>
<sup>9</sup> For thou, LORD, _art_ high above all the earth:<br>Thou art exalted far above all gods.<br>
<sup>10</sup> Ye that love the LORD, hate evil:<br>He preserveth the souls of his saints; He delivereth them out of the hand of the wicked.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Light is sown for the righteous,<br>And gladness for the upright in heart.<br>
<sup>12</sup> Rejoice in the LORD, ye righteous;<br>And give thanks at the remembrance<sup>c</sup> of his holiness.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Heb. _many_, or, _great Isles_
> - <sup>b</sup> 2. Or, _establishment_
> - <sup>c</sup> 12. Or, _to the memorial_

## Chapter 98

> _A Psalm._

<sup>1</sup> O sing unto the LORD a new song;<br>For he hath done marvellous things:<br>His right hand, and his holy arm,<br>Hath gotten him the victory.<br>
<sup>2</sup> The LORD hath made known his salvation:<br>His righteousness hath he openly shewed in the sight of the heathen.<br>
<sup>3</sup> He hath remembered his mercy and his truth toward the house of Israel:<br>All the ends of the earth have seen the salvation of our God.<br>
<sup>4</sup> Make a joyful noise unto the LORD, all the earth:<br>Make a loud noise, and rejoice, and sing praise.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Sing unto the LORD with the harp;<br>With the harp, and the voice of a psalm.<br>
<sup>6</sup> With trumpets and sound of cornet<br>Make a joyful noise before the LORD, the King.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Let the sea roar, and the fulness thereof;<br>The world, and they that dwell therein.<br>
<sup>8</sup> Let the floods clap _their_ hands: Let the hills be joyful together<br>
<sup>9</sup> Before the LORD;<br>For he cometh to judge the earth:<br>With righteousness shall he judge the world,<br>And the people with equity.<br>

## Chapter 99

<sup>1</sup> The LORD reigneth; let the people tremble:<br>He sitteth _between_ the cherubims; let the earth be moved.<br>
<sup>2</sup> The LORD _is_ great in Zion;<br>And he _is_ high above all the people.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Let them praise thy great and terrible name;<br>_For_ it _is_ holy.<br>
<sup>4</sup> The king’s strength also loveth judgment; Thou dost establish equity,<br>Thou executest judgment and righteousness in Jacob.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Exalt ye the LORD our God,<br>And worship at his footstool; _For_ he _is_ holy.<sup>a</sup><br>
<sup>6</sup> Moses and Aaron among his priests, and Samuel among them that call upon his name;<br>They called upon the LORD, and he answered them.<br>
<sup>7</sup> He spake unto them in the cloudy pillar:<br>They kept his testimonies, and the ordinance _that_ he gave them.<br>
<sup>8</sup> Thou answeredst them, O LORD our God:<br>Thou wast a God that forgavest them, though thou tookest vengeance of their inventions.<br>
<sup>9</sup> Exalt the LORD our God, and worship at his holy hill;<br>For the LORD our God _is_ holy.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 5. Or, _it is holy_

## Chapter 100

> _A Psalm of praise._

<sup>1</sup> Make a joyful noise unto the LORD, all ye lands.<sup>a</sup><br>
<sup>2</sup> Serve the LORD with gladness: Come before his presence with singing.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Know ye that the LORD he _is_ God:<br>_It is_ he _that_ hath made us, and not we ourselves;<sup>b</sup> _We are_ his people, and the sheep of his pasture.<br>
<sup>4</sup> Enter into his gates with thanksgiving, _And_ into his courts with praise:<br>Be thankful unto him, _and_ bless his name.<br>
<sup>5</sup> For the LORD _is_ good; his mercy _is_ everlasting;<br>And his truth _endureth_ to all generations.<sup>c</sup><br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Heb. _all the earth_
> - <sup>b</sup> 3. Or, _and his we are_
> - <sup>c</sup> 5. Heb. _to generation, and generation_

## Chapter 101

> _A Psalm of David._

<sup>1</sup> I will sing of mercy and judgment:<br>Unto thee, O LORD, will I sing.<br>
<sup>2</sup> I will behave myself wisely<br>In a perfect way.<br>O when wilt thou come unto me?<br>I will walk within my house with a perfect heart.<br>
<sup>3</sup> I will set no wicked thing<sup>a</sup> before mine eyes: I hate the work of them that turn aside;<br>_It_ shall not cleave to me.<br>
<sup>4</sup> A froward heart shall depart from me:<br>I will not know a wicked _person_.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Whoso privily slandereth his neighbour,<br>Him will I cut off:<br>Him that hath an high look<br>And a proud heart will not I suffer.<br>
<sup>6</sup> Mine eyes _shall be_ upon the faithful<br>Of the land, that they may dwell with me:<br>He that walketh in a perfect way,<sup>b</sup><br>He shall serve me.<br>
<sup>7</sup> He that worketh deceit shall not dwell within my house:<br>He that telleth lies shall not tarry<sup>c</sup> in my sight.<br>
<sup>8</sup> I will early destroy all the wicked of the land; That I may cut off<br>All wicked doers from the city of the LORD.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 3. Heb. _thing of Belial_
> - <sup>b</sup> 6. Or, _perfect in the way_
> - <sup>c</sup> 7. Heb. _shall not be established_

## Chapter 102

> _A Prayer of the afflicted, when he is overwhelmed, and poureth out his complaint before the LORD._

<sup>1</sup> Hear my prayer, O LORD,<br>And let my cry come unto thee.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Hide not thy face from me in the day _when_ I am in trouble;<br>Incline thine ear unto me: In the day _when_ I call answer me speedily.<br>
<sup>3</sup> For my days are consumed like smoke,<sup>a</sup><br>And my bones are burned as an hearth.<br>
<sup>4</sup> My heart is smitten, and withered like grass;<br>So that I forget to eat my bread.<br>
<sup>5</sup> By reason of the voice of my groaning<br>My bones cleave to my skin.<sup>b</sup><br>
<sup>6</sup> I am like a pelican of the wilderness:<br>I am like an owl of the desert.<br>
<sup>7</sup> I watch, and am as a sparrow<br>Alone upon the house top.<br>
<sup>8</sup> Mine enemies reproach me all the day;<br>_And_ they that are mad against me are sworn against me.<br>
<sup>9</sup> For I have eaten ashes like bread,<br>And mingled my drink with weeping,<br>
<sup>10</sup> Because of thine indignation and thy wrath:<br>For thou hast lifted me up, and cast me down.<br>
<sup>11</sup> My days _are_ like a shadow that declineth;<br>And I am withered like grass.<br>
<sup>12</sup> But thou, O LORD, shalt endure for ever;<br>And thy remembrance unto all generations.<br>
<sup>13</sup> Thou shalt arise, _and_ have mercy upon Zion:<br>For the time to favour her, yea, the set time, is come.<br>
<sup>14</sup> For thy servants take pleasure in her stones,<br>And favour the dust thereof.<br>
<sup>15</sup> So the heathen shall fear the name of the LORD,<br>And all the kings of the earth thy glory.<br>
<sup>16</sup> When the LORD shall build up Zion,<br>He shall appear in his glory.<br>
<sup>17</sup> He will regard the prayer of the destitute,<br>And not despise their prayer.<br>
<sup>18</sup> This shall be written for the generation to come:<br>And the people which shall be created shall praise the LORD.<br>
<sup>19</sup> For he hath looked down from the height of his sanctuary;<br>From heaven did the LORD behold the earth;<br>
<sup>20</sup> To hear the groaning of the prisoner;<br>To loose those that are appointed to death;<sup>c</sup><br>
<sup>21</sup> To declare the name of the LORD in Zion,<br>And his praise in Jerusalem;<br>
<sup>22</sup> When the people are gathered together,<br>And the kingdoms, to serve the LORD.<br>
<sup>23</sup> He weakened<sup>d</sup> my strength in the way;<br>He shortened my days.<br>
<sup>24</sup> I said, O my God, take me not away in the midst of my days:<br>Thy years _are_ throughout all generations.<br>
<sup>25</sup> Of old hast thou laid the foundation of the earth:<br>And the heavens _are_ the work of thy hands.<br>
<sup>26</sup> They shall perish, but thou shalt endure:<sup>e</sup><br>Yea, all of them shall wax old like a garment;<br>As a vesture shalt thou change them, and they shall be changed:<br>
<sup>27</sup> But thou _art_ the same, and thy years shall have no end.<br>
<sup>28</sup> The children of thy servants shall continue,<br>And their seed shall be established before thee.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 3. Or, (as some read) _into smoke_
> - <sup>b</sup> 5. Or, _flesh_
> - <sup>c</sup> 20. Heb. _the children of death_
> - <sup>d</sup> 23. Heb. _afflicted_
> - <sup>e</sup> 26. Heb. _stand_

## Chapter 103

> __A Psalm_ of David._

<sup>1</sup> Bless the LORD, O my soul:<br>And all that is within me, _bless_ his holy name.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Bless the LORD, O my soul,<br>And forget not all his benefits:<br>
<sup>3</sup> Who forgiveth all thine iniquities;<br>Who healeth all thy diseases;<br>
<sup>4</sup> Who redeemeth thy life from destruction;<br>Who crowneth thee with lovingkindness and tender mercies;<br>
<sup>5</sup> Who satisfieth thy mouth with good _things_;<br>_So that_ thy youth is renewed like the eagle’s.<br>
<sup>6</sup> The LORD executeth righteousness and judgment<br>For all that are oppressed.<br>
<sup>7</sup> He made known his ways unto Moses,<br>His acts unto the children of Israel.<br>
<sup>8</sup> The LORD _is_ merciful and gracious,<br>Slow to anger, and plenteous in mercy.<sup>a</sup><br>
<sup>9</sup> He will not always chide:<br>Neither will he keep _his anger_ for ever.<br>
<sup>10</sup> He hath not dealt with us after our sins;<br>Nor rewarded us according to our iniquities.<br>
<sup>11</sup> For as the heaven is high<sup>b</sup> above the earth,<br>_So_ great is his mercy toward them that fear him.<br>
<sup>12</sup> As far as the east is from the west,<br>_So_ far hath he removed our transgressions from us.<br>
<sup>13</sup> Like as a father pitieth _his_ children,<br>_So_ the LORD pitieth them that fear him.<br>
<sup>14</sup> For he knoweth our frame;<br>He remembereth that we _are_ dust.<br>
<sup>15</sup> _As for_ man, his days _are_ as grass:<br>As a flower of the field, so he flourisheth.<br>
<sup>16</sup> For the wind passeth over it, and it is gone;<sup>c</sup><br>And the place thereof shall know it no more.<br>
<sup>17</sup> But the mercy of the LORD _is_ from everlasting to everlasting upon them that fear him,<br>And his righteousness unto children’s children;<br>
<sup>18</sup> To such as keep his covenant,<br>And to those that remember his commandments to do them.<br>
<sup>19</sup> The LORD hath prepared his throne in the heavens;<br>And his kingdom ruleth over all.<br>
<sup>20</sup> Bless the LORD, ye his angels, that excel in strength,<sup>d</sup><br>That do his commandments, hearkening unto the voice of his word.<br>
<sup>21</sup> Bless ye the LORD, all _ye_ his hosts;<br>_Ye_ ministers of his, that do his pleasure.<br>
<sup>22</sup> Bless the LORD, all his works in all places of his dominion:<br>Bless the LORD, O my soul.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 8. Heb. _great of mercy_
> - <sup>b</sup> 11. Heb. _according to the height of the heaven_
> - <sup>c</sup> 16. Heb. _it is not_
> - <sup>d</sup> 20. Heb. _mighty in strength_

## Chapter 104

<sup>1</sup> Bless the LORD, O my soul.<br>O LORD my God, thou art very great; Thou art clothed with honour and majesty:<br>
<sup>2</sup> Who coverest _thyself_ with light as _with_ a garment:<br>Who stretchest out the heavens like a curtain:<br>
<sup>3</sup> Who layeth the beams of his chambers in the waters:<br>Who maketh the clouds his chariot: Who walketh upon the wings of the wind:<br>
<sup>4</sup> Who maketh his angels spirits;<br>His ministers a flaming fire:<br>
<sup>5</sup> _Who_ laid the foundations of the earth,<sup>a</sup><br>_That_ it should not be removed for ever.<br>
<sup>6</sup> Thou coveredst it with the deep as _with_ a garment:<br>The waters stood above the mountains.<br>
<sup>7</sup> At thy rebuke they fled;<br>At the voice of thy thunder they hasted away.<br>
<sup>8</sup> They go up by the mountains;<sup>b</sup> they go down by the valleys<br>Unto the place which thou hast founded for them.<br>
<sup>9</sup> Thou hast set a bound that they may not pass over;<br>That they turn not again to cover the earth.<br>
<sup>10</sup> He sendeth<sup>c</sup> the springs into the valleys,<br>_Which_ run<sup>d</sup> among the hills.<br>
<sup>11</sup> They give drink to every beast of the field:<br>The wild asses quench<sup>e</sup> their thirst.<br>
<sup>12</sup> By them shall the fowls of the heaven have their habitation,<br>_Which_ sing<sup>f</sup> among the branches.<br>
<sup>13</sup> He watereth the hills from his chambers:<br>The earth is satisfied with the fruit of thy works.<br>
<sup>14</sup> He causeth the grass to grow for the cattle,<br>And herb for the service of man:<br>That he may bring forth food out of the earth;
<sup>15</sup> And wine _that_ maketh glad the heart of man,<br>_And_ oil to make _his_ face to shine,<sup>g</sup> and bread _which_ strengtheneth man’s heart.<br>
<sup>16</sup> The trees of the LORD are full _of sap_;<br>The cedars of Lebanon, which he hath planted;<br>
<sup>17</sup> Where the birds make their nests:<br>_As for_ the stork, the fir trees _are_ her house.<br>
<sup>18</sup> The high hills _are_ a refuge for the wild goats;<br>_And_ the rocks for the conies.<br>
<sup>19</sup> He appointed the moon for seasons:<br>The sun knoweth his going down.<br>
<sup>20</sup> Thou makest darkness, and it is night:<br>Wherein all the beasts of the forest do creep _forth_.<sup>h</sup><br>
<sup>21</sup> The young lions roar after their prey,<br>And seek their meat from God.<br>
<sup>22</sup> The sun ariseth, they gather themselves together,<br>And lay them down in their dens.<br>
<sup>23</sup> Man goeth forth unto his work<br>And to his labour until the evening.<br>
<sup>24</sup> O LORD, how manifold are thy works!<br>In wisdom hast thou made them all: The earth is full of thy riches.<br>
<sup>25</sup> _So is_ this great and wide sea,<br>Wherein _are_ things creeping innumerable, both small and great beasts.<br>
<sup>26</sup> There go the ships: _There is_ that leviathan,<br>_Whom_ thou hast made<sup>i</sup> to play therein.<br>
<sup>27</sup> These wait all upon thee;<br>That thou mayest give _them_ their meat in due season.<br>
<sup>28</sup> _That_ thou givest them they gather:<br>Thou openest thine hand, they are filled with good.<br>
<sup>29</sup> Thou hidest thy face, they are troubled:<br>Thou takest away their breath, they die, and return to their dust.<br>
<sup>30</sup> Thou sendest forth thy spirit, they are created:<br>And thou renewest the face of the earth.<br>
<sup>31</sup> The glory of the LORD shall endure<sup>j</sup> for ever:<br>The LORD shall rejoice in his works.<br>
<sup>32</sup> He looketh on the earth, and it trembleth:<br>He toucheth the hills, and they smoke.<br>
<sup>33</sup> I will sing unto the LORD as long as I live:<br>I will sing praise to my God while I have my being.<br>
<sup>34</sup> My meditation of him shall be sweet:<br>I will be glad in the LORD.<br>
<sup>35</sup> Let the sinners be consumed out of the earth, and let the wicked be no more.<br>Bless thou the LORD, O my soul.<br>Praise ye the LORD.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 5. Heb. _he hath founded the earth upon her bases_
> - <sup>b</sup> 8. Or, _The mountains ascend, the valleys descend_
> - <sup>c</sup> 10. Heb. _who sendeth_
> - <sup>d</sup> 10. Heb. _walk_
> - <sup>e</sup> 11. Heb. _break_
> - <sup>f</sup> 12. Heb. _give a voice_
> - <sup>g</sup> 15. Heb. _to make his face shine with oil_, or _more then oil_
> - <sup>h</sup> 20. Heb. _all the beasts thereof do trample on the forest_
> - <sup>i</sup> 26. Heb. _formed_
> - <sup>j</sup> 31. Heb. _shall be_

## Chapter 105

<sup>1</sup> O give thanks unto the LORD; call upon his name:<br>Make known his deeds among the people.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Sing unto him, sing psalms unto him:<br>Talk ye of all his wondrous works.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Glory ye in his holy name:<br>Let the heart of them rejoice that seek the LORD.<br>
<sup>4</sup> Seek the LORD, and his strength:<br>Seek his face evermore.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Remember his marvellous works that he hath done;<br>His wonders, and the judgments of his mouth;<br>
<sup>6</sup> O ye seed of Abraham his servant,<br>Ye children of Jacob his chosen.<br>
<sup>7</sup> He _is_ the LORD our God:<br>His judgments _are_ in all the earth.<br>
<sup>8</sup> He hath remembered his covenant for ever,<br>The word _which_ he commanded to a thousand generations.<br>
<sup>9</sup> Which _covenant_ he made with Abraham,<br>And his oath unto Isaac;<br>
<sup>10</sup> And confirmed the same unto Jacob for a law,<br>_And_ to Israel _for_ an everlasting covenant:<br>
<sup>11</sup> Saying, Unto thee will I give the land of Canaan,<br>The lot<sup>a</sup> of your inheritance:<br>
<sup>12</sup> When they were _but_ a few men in number;<br>Yea, very few, and strangers in it.<br>
<sup>13</sup> When they went from one nation to another,<br>From _one_ kingdom to another people;<br>
<sup>14</sup> He suffered no man to do them wrong:<br>Yea, he reproved kings for their sakes;<br>
<sup>15</sup> _Saying_, Touch not mine anointed,<br>And do my prophets no harm.<br>
<sup>16</sup> Moreover he called for a famine upon the land:<br>He brake the whole staff of bread.<br>
<sup>17</sup> He sent a man before them,<br>_Even_ Joseph, _who_ was sold for a servant:<br>
<sup>18</sup> Whose feet they hurt with fetters:<br>He was laid in iron:<sup>b</sup><br>
<sup>19</sup> Until the time that his word came:<br>The word of the LORD tried him.<br>
<sup>20</sup> The king sent and loosed him;<br>_Even_ the ruler of the people, and let him go free.<br>
<sup>21</sup> He made him lord of his house,<br>And ruler of all his substance:<sup>c</sup><br>
<sup>22</sup> To bind his princes at his pleasure;<br>And teach his senators wisdom.<br>
<sup>23</sup> Israel also came into Egypt;<br>And Jacob sojourned in the land of Ham.<br>
<sup>24</sup> And he increased his people greatly;<br>And made them stronger than their enemies.<br>
<sup>25</sup> He turned their heart to hate his people,<br>To deal subtilly with his servants.<br>
<sup>26</sup> He sent Moses his servant;<br>_And_ Aaron whom he had chosen.<br>
<sup>27</sup> They shewed his signs<sup>d</sup> among them,<br>And wonders in the land of Ham.<br>
<sup>28</sup> He sent darkness, and made it dark;<br>And they rebelled not against his word.<br>
<sup>29</sup> He turned their waters into blood,<br>And slew their fish.<br>
<sup>30</sup> Their land brought forth frogs in abundance,<br>In the chambers of their kings.<br>
<sup>31</sup> He spake, and there came divers sorts of flies,<br>_And_ lice in all their coasts.<br>
<sup>32</sup> He gave them hail for rain,<sup>e</sup><br>_And_ flaming fire in their land.<br>
<sup>33</sup> He smote their vines also and their fig trees;<br>And brake the trees of their coasts.<br>
<sup>34</sup> He spake, and the locusts came, and caterpillers, and that without number,<br>
<sup>35</sup> And did eat up all the herbs in their land, and devoured the fruit of their ground.<br>
<sup>36</sup> He smote also all the firstborn in their land,<br>The chief of all their strength.<br>
<sup>37</sup> He brought them forth also with silver and gold:<br>And _there was_ not one feeble _person_ among their tribes.<br>
<sup>38</sup> Egypt was glad when they departed:<br>For the fear of them fell upon them.<br>
<sup>39</sup> He spread a cloud for a covering;<br>And fire to give light in the night.<br>
<sup>40</sup> _The people_ asked, and he brought quails,<br>And satisfied them with the bread of heaven.<br>
<sup>41</sup> He opened the rock, and the waters gushed out;<br>They ran in the dry places _like_ a river.<br>
<sup>42</sup> For he remembered his holy promise,<br>_And_ Abraham his servant.<br>
<sup>43</sup> And he brought forth his people with joy,<br>_And_ his chosen with gladness:<sup>f</sup><br>
<sup>44</sup> And gave them the lands of the heathen:<br>And they inherited the labour of the people;<br>
<sup>45</sup> That they might observe his statutes,<br>And keep his laws.<br>Praise ye the LORD.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 11. Heb. _the cord_
> - <sup>b</sup> 18. Heb. _his soul came into iron_
> - <sup>c</sup> 21. Heb. _possession_
> - <sup>d</sup> 27. Heb. _words of his signs_
> - <sup>e</sup> 32. Heb. _he gave their rain, hail_
> - <sup>f</sup> 43. Heb. _singing_

## Chapter 106

<sup>1</sup> Praise ye<sup>a</sup> the LORD. O give thanks unto the LORD; for _he is_ good:<br>For his mercy _endureth_ for ever.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Who can utter the mighty acts of the LORD?<br>_Who_ can shew forth all his praise?<br>
<sup>3</sup> Blessed _are_ they that keep judgment,<br>_And_ he that doeth righteousness at all times.<br>
<sup>4</sup> Remember me, O LORD, with the favour _that thou bearest unto_ thy people:<br>O visit me with thy salvation;<br>
<sup>5</sup> That I may see the good of thy chosen,<br>That I may rejoice in the gladness of thy nation, that I may glory with thine inheritance.<br>
<sup>6</sup> We have sinned with our fathers,<br>We have committed iniquity, we have done wickedly.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Our fathers understood not thy wonders in Egypt; They remembered not the multitude of thy mercies;<br>But provoked _him_ at the sea, _even_ at the Red sea.<br>
<sup>8</sup> Nevertheless he saved them for his name’s sake,<br>That he might make his mighty power to be known.<br>
<sup>9</sup> He rebuked the Red sea also, and it was dried up:<br>So he led them through the depths, as through the wilderness.<br>
<sup>10</sup> And he saved them from the hand of him that hated _them_,<br>And redeemed them from the hand of the enemy.<br>
<sup>11</sup> And the waters covered their enemies:<br>There was not one of them left.<br>
<sup>12</sup> Then believed they his words;<br>They sang his praise.<br>
<sup>13</sup> They soon forgat<sup>b</sup> his works;<br>They waited not for his counsel:<br>
<sup>14</sup> But lusted exceedingly<sup>c</sup> in the wilderness,<br>And tempted God in the desert.<br>
<sup>15</sup> And he gave them their request;<br>But sent leanness into their soul.<br>
<sup>16</sup> They envied Moses also in the camp,<br>_And_ Aaron the saint of the LORD.<br>
<sup>17</sup> The earth opened and swallowed up Dathan,<br>And covered the company of Abiram.<br>
<sup>18</sup> And a fire was kindled in their company;<br>The flame burned up the wicked.<br>
<sup>19</sup> They made a calf in Horeb,<br>And worshipped the molten image.<br>
<sup>20</sup> Thus they changed their glory<br>Into the similitude of an ox that eateth grass.<br>
<sup>21</sup> They forgat God their saviour,<br>Which had done great things in Egypt;<br>
<sup>22</sup> Wondrous works in the land of Ham,<br>_And_ terrible things by the Red sea.<br>
<sup>23</sup> Therefore he said that he would destroy them, had not Moses his chosen stood before him in the breach,<br>To turn away his wrath, lest he should destroy _them_.<br>
<sup>24</sup> Yea, they despised the pleasant land,<sup>d</sup><br>They believed not his word:<br>
<sup>25</sup> But murmured in their tents,<br>_And_ hearkened not unto the voice of the LORD.<br>
<sup>26</sup> Therefore he lifted up his hand against them,<br>To overthrow them in the wilderness:<br>
<sup>27</sup> To overthrow<sup>e</sup> their seed also among the nations,<br>And to scatter them in the lands.<br>
<sup>28</sup> They joined themselves also unto Baalpeor,<br>And ate the sacrifices of the dead.<br>
<sup>29</sup> Thus they provoked _him_ to anger with their inventions:<br>And the plague brake in upon them.<br>
<sup>30</sup> Then stood up Phinehas, and executed judgment:<br>And _so_ the plague was stayed.<br>
<sup>31</sup> And that was counted unto him for righteousness<br>Unto all generations for evermore.<br>
<sup>32</sup> They angered _him_ also at the waters of strife,<br>So that it went ill with Moses for their sakes:<br>
<sup>33</sup> Because they provoked his spirit,<br>So that he spake unadvisedly with his lips.<br>
<sup>34</sup> They did not destroy the nations,<br>Concerning whom the LORD commanded them:<br>
<sup>35</sup> But were mingled among<br>the heathen, and learned their works.<br>
<sup>36</sup> And they served their idols: Which were a snare unto them.<br>
<sup>37</sup> Yea, they sacrificed their sons and their daughters unto devils,<br>
